Company - Competitive advantage

C707 questions
C-4178C-4270C-4372C-4480C-4530C-4685C-4762

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

The competitive advantage is top-decile and demonstrable on cost, trust, scale, licence and culture, it widens at home with each period, and it produces financial outputs that pass every numeric test the framework sets by a wide margin. It has one structural gap, pricing power, which the model promise rules out, and two narrowing edges, regional scale against Nordnet and yield at the affluent end, both of which Q3 2026 made visible in the income statement for the first time. For the Consumer, the advantage justifies carrying volume lines (customers, savings capital, inflows, churn) at or near current rates with high confidence over four years, carrying costs at the guided rate, and carrying the revenue margin as the one line in which the advantage does not protect the forecast.

C-41

Sustainable relative advantages: which, how strong, widening or narrowing?

B78

Question asked Which sustainable relative competitive advantages or moats does the company hold (customer-related; production-related; scale-based; regulatory or political; culture or knowledge), how strong is each, and are they widening or narrowing?

Judgement. Avanza holds demonstrable advantages on all five of the framework's dimensions; they are very strong at home, widening on the installed base and narrowing on two edges (regional scale and yield at the top end).

  • Customer-related: the strongest consumer financial brand in Sweden (16 consecutive Swedish Quality Index wins; satisfaction 77.6 against 70.0; NPS 41 against an industry 3; Sweden's highest-considered bank in Verian's index), converted into fee and funding-cost insulation (customers show lower rate sensitivity because they trust the platform; 93% of cash is paid interest but at an average 0.79%), multi-product switching costs (pensions, mortgages, ISK and endowment wrappers), and churn of 0.7%. Widening: churn down from 0.9%, share up.
  • Production-related: one Java stack with a uniform API framework, instant central deployment, 25 product teams, 450,000 to 490,000 daily active users on a highly automated interface; cost-to-savings 14 basis points against Nordnet's 15 to 16 and banks far higher; development expensed. Stable, with caveats: technical debt, outages under load, a second platform to come.
  • Scale-based: 20.4% of Nasdaq Stockholm transactions enabling internal matching and faster execution; volume leverage over fund manufacturers for rebates; 2.39m customers in a population of 10.5m. Widening at home; narrowing regionally.
  • Regulatory: a bank, a life insurer and a fund company under FSA supervision; BankID, ISK and DORA compliance that sub-scale and foreign entrants must bear; the same regulator is the fragility.
  • Culture and knowledge: eNPS 57, leadership score 86, staff turnover 9%, named by the framework as an exemplar; the C-suite churn of 2025 is the outlier against it. Narrowing: at the top end, where Private Banking and Pro customers get cheaper currency and brokerage, and where SAVR and Montrose price below; and regionally, where Nordnet's single platform across four markets lowers unit cost each period. The advantage is on cost, trust and scale; it is not on price, and the thesis does not need it to be.
C-42

Scale relative to competitors and other actors, and what it confers

C70

Question asked How large is the company's scale relative to competitors and other actors (revenue, headcount and so on), and what does that scale confer?

Judgement. Very large at home, second in the region, mid-sized in Europe; the scale confers the cost ratio, the order-flow advantage and the brand, and it does not confer regional unit-cost leadership. Revenue SEK 4,495m (2025) against Nordnet SEK 5,389m, flatexDEGIRO EUR 560m, Fineco EUR 1,320m; customers 2.39m against Nordnet 2.57m; savings capital SEK 1,277bn against 1,459bn; employees 812 against a far larger Fineco adviser network (3,076) and a Nordnet of comparable size. At home: twice Nordnet's savings share and the largest exchange participant. What scale confers: a 14 basis point cost ratio (the lowest in the set), a 69% incremental margin on each krona of capital, internal order matching, rebate leverage, and a marketing cost that is largely word of mouth (acquisition cost about SEK 590). What it does not confer: the pan-Nordic fixed-cost absorption that gives Nordnet a 76% Danish operating margin and lets it spread technology and regulatory cost across four regimes.

C-43

Realistic total addressable market relative to the company, and the mid-term growth it allows

C72

Question asked How large is the realistic total addressable market relative to the company, and what rate of growth does it allow for over the mid term?

Judgement. Large relative to the company and growing: the realistic addressable market allows 10% to 15% a year savings-capital growth over the mid term without any share gain in Denmark. The Swedish savings market is SEK 13,900bn and has grown 7% a year for a decade; Avanza holds 8.7%. Its own customers keep about two-thirds of their wealth elsewhere, roughly half of which management considers addressable, so the in-base opportunity is of the order of SEK 1,200bn, roughly equal to current capital. The non-collectively-agreed occupational pension market is SEK 1,011bn to 1,062bn, of which Avanza has about 11% of premiums and pension savers are only 9% of its customers. Traditional discretionary management (Sigma's target) is SEK 1,000bn. Denmark adds 70% on the company's sizing (SEK 6.2trn to 9trn), with the caveat that much of it sits in institutional pension vehicles Avanza cannot reach at launch. Growth allowed: market return (say 5% to 7%) plus net inflow (4% to 8%) gives 10% to 15% a year, which is the company's own target range; the binding constraint is not the market but the share of wallet and the leverage ratio on the deposit part of it.

C-44

Surplus customer utility: leading, or at least not inferior?

B80

Question asked How high is the company's surplus customer utility against competing or alternative offerings, and is it leading, or at least demonstrably not inferior? (Hard to prove, easier to disprove; the aim is to disprove inferiority at Early-Stage and Deep Dive, for example Sophos, Bytes.)

Judgement. Leading in Sweden on every measured dimension, and non-inferiority is easy to hold; the one place Nordnet leads is breadth (Shareville's 500,000-plus members, four markets, 43% cross-border trades, more venues), and the one place challengers lead is price at the affluent end. Evidence: 16 years at the top of the Swedish Quality Index; satisfaction 77.6 against 70.0; NPS 41 against 3; highest brand consideration; search volume far ahead of Nordnet on Jefferies' reading; flow share above stock share; churn 0.7%; fifth most attractive employer in Sweden. Product: 80,000-plus instruments, 1,300-plus funds, pre- and post-market US trading, free real-time US quotes, research hub, Sigma, Pension Portal, 85% loan-to-value mortgages. The disproof test (is the offering inferior anywhere?) fails only on breadth and on price for the top cohort, which is exactly where the Q3 yield compression came from. For a model this is the reason churn and inflows can be carried at current rates with confidence.

C-45

Demonstrable pricing power with minimal utility erosion, shown by application

❌ D30

Question asked To what extent does the company have, and use, demonstrable pricing power with minimal erosion of its relative surplus customer utility, shown by evidence of application rather than conjecture?

Judgement. Not demonstrated, and structurally unlikely to be. The evidence of application runs the other way: the brokerage yield fell to 10.3 basis points, the fund margin to 22.2, currency conversion is half price for the affluent, and management has introduced cheaper fee classes and a 17 basis point index fund. The only prices applied upward are the mortgage rate (+10 basis points for the SEK 3m to 10m tier, alongside a pause) and the pass-through of rate cuts to deposit rates (the funding-side price, used quickly and at 80% on savings accounts). The 25 basis point foreign-exchange spread for standard customers has held, which is the one hidden fee with evidence of holding. Pricing power exists in the framework's sense only through mix (foreign trading lifting the blended rate) and retention. The Consumer should carry zero price contribution and a negative drift.

C-46

Sharing scale economies with customers: stated or de facto

B85

Question asked To what extent does the company share its scale economies with customers, stated or de facto, in its messaging, strategy, culture or values?

Judgement. Explicitly and consistently, in strategy, messaging and action: zero platform fees for retail savers, an index fund covering 99% of the Stockholm exchange at 17 basis points, commission-free trading up to SEK 50,000 for new customers, free pre-filled tax forms, free real-time US data, Sigma at half the incumbent price, educational campaigns on how fees erode returns, and the "Customers first. Always." principle. The sharing is the moat's engine (it drives flow share, word-of-mouth acquisition at low cost and churn under 1%) and the reason unit pricing cannot rise. It is also the reason the business is congruent: the cost ratio of 14 basis points is what makes a 45 basis point revenue margin with a 69% operating margin possible while prices fall.

C-47

Gravitational forces improving advantage and outputs each period, against peers

C62

Question asked To what extent do gravitational or mechanical forces, rather than heroic company dynamism, improve the company's competitive advantage and its financial outputs against peers with each period that passes?

Judgement. Mechanical forces run in Avanza's favour at home and against it regionally, and in 2026 the mechanical improvement in financial outputs is being partly absorbed by the equally mechanical drift in yield. In its favour: every new customer adds brand, data and order flow at near-zero marginal cost; each year of capital growth lowers the cost ratio (14.5 to 14.1 to about 14 basis points); the flow share above stock share compounds the base; the 69% incremental margin on new capital means outputs improve with volume without heroics; the deposit internalisation re-based NII permanently. Against it: Nordnet's four-market platform compounds unit cost advantages faster; and the mix of what customers buy (index funds), who trades (the affluent at lower rates) and how they pay for currency (12.5 basis points) drifts the revenue margin down about 1 basis point a year, so volume must grow about 2% to 3% a year just to stand still on income. Q3 2026 is the first quarter in which the second force visibly outran the first (savings capital +21%, income +12%). Against peers the outputs still improve (ROE 44%, margin 72%), but the lead over Nordnet on growth is gone and on margin is a matter of accounting basis.

Company - Economic strength

C6811 questions
C-4960C-5058C-5185C-5280C-5375C-5478C-7560C-7680C-7745C-7862C-7972

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

The economics are those of a top-decile franchise: returns on equity above 40% with no capital need, earnings that are cash, the cleanest accounting in its peer set, a 68% to 72% operating margin, and a tax rate of 14.5%. The growth is mid-teens in the delivered years and high single digits to low double digits in the forecast years, and it is cyclical in roughly half its revenue. The operating leverage is high and symmetrical in arithmetic but asymmetrical in practice, because costs are guided to grow 8% to 9% whatever revenue does and management reinvests rather than cuts, so a normalisation of trading shows up fully in profit. For the Consumer: build the model on savings capital, a revenue margin by line, a cost line at the guide, a 14.5% tax rate, an AT1 coupon below the line and 0.4% a year of dilution; hold return on equity at about 40%; and treat the revenue margin and the transactional lines as the two inputs on which the whole forecast error concentrates.

C-49

EPS growth, revenue growth, and durability

C60

Question asked What earnings-per-share growth is the company generating, with what revenue growth, and how durable is it?

Judgement. Avanza is generating mid-teens EPS growth on mid-teens revenue growth in 2025 and 2026, decelerating to high single digits on consensus for 2027 and 2028; about half of the growth is durable (the asset-scaled lines) and the other half is cyclical and has just turned. The right modelling rate for the base case is 9% to 12% a year EPS growth over the four years, with wide bands.

Delivered. FY2025: operating income +15% to SEK 4,495m, operating profit +17% to SEK 3,078m, EPS +15.6% to SEK 16.57. Nine months 2026: income +15% to SEK 3,874m, operating profit +16% to SEK 2,727m, EPS +15% to SEK 14.58. By quarter EPS: Q1 2026 SEK 4.71 (+5%), Q2 4.97 (+30%), Q3 4.90 (+12%). Longer: EPS compounded 33% a year from FY2019 (SEK 2.94) to FY2025 but only 5.9% a year from the FY2021 boom (SEK 13.19), with FY2022 down 19%, which is the measure of how cyclical the transactional half is.

Consensus (13 October, pre-Q3). EPS SEK 19.40 (FY2026, +17%), 20.87 (+7.6%), 22.88 (+9.6%), 24.60 (FY2029, one broker); revenue SEK 5,225m (+16%), 5,726m (+9.6%), 6,216m (+8.6%). DNB Carnegie after Q3: SEK 18.90, 19.29, 20.79. The three-year EPS compound rate FY2025 to FY2028 is 11.4% on the median.

Durability. NII (33%) and fund commissions (17%) are earned on a base that compounds with savings capital and deposits; brokerage (28%) and currency (16%) are earned on turnover and the foreign share, both of which peaked in H1 2026. The recurring half is durable at 8% to 12% a year on the volume evidence; the transactional half can fall 10% to 20% in a normalisation year (2022 precedent). Net: durable at a lower rate than delivered, with the H1 2026 velocity not repeatable in the base case.

C-50

Scope to deploy capital or operating spend at high return for three to five years, and the fair multiple

C58

Question asked How much immediate scope is there to deploy capital or operating spend at a high return on new invested capital for three to five years, and what does that mean for mid- and long-term potential and the fair multiple?

Judgement. The business needs almost no capital to grow (capital expenditure SEK 74m in 2025, 1.6% of income; consensus SEK 63m to 90m a year), so the reinvestment question is about operating spend, where the scope is real but modest, and the return on the base business is far above any hurdle while the return on the new spend is unproven.

On the base: each marginal krona of savings capital earns a 45 basis point revenue margin against a 14 basis point cost, and each internalised deposit earns about 50 basis points against 5 before, so the incremental return on the base franchise is of the order of the 40% to 44% return on equity. On the new spend: Denmark (SEK 120m to 150m pre-launch, about SEK 140m a year after, break-even about 2032), Sigma (unproven economics), pensions (recurring, slow), cloud (a cost of staying in business); the sum is 5% to 6% of the cost base a year and management's own expectation is five years to profit on the largest piece. The fair-multiple implication: a high-return business with limited reinvestment need distributes rather than compounds, so the multiple is set by growth and durability, not by return on new capital; that is why the market pays Nordnet for growth and discounts Avanza. A 17x to 20x two-year-forward multiple is fair on this evidence; the stock is at 16.2x to 16.7x.

C-51

Returns on capital: post-tax return on new invested capital, on capital employed, on tangible capital

B85

Question asked How high are the returns on capital: post-tax return on new invested capital, on capital employed and on tangible capital?

Judgement. Exceptional and the best in the set on a like-for-like basis. Return on equity 40% in 2025, 42% in H1 2026, 43% in Q2, 44% in Q3, against a 35% minimum target; equity of about SEK 9,500m on consensus FY2026 against intangibles of SEK 21m (Sigmastocks) and right-of-use assets of SEK 339m, so return on tangible equity is effectively the same figure. Return on new invested capital on the base franchise is above the return on equity (C-50). Peers: Nordnet 38% to 42% (overstated by software capitalisation of SEK 223m of personnel cost), Fineco 25%, flatexDEGIRO 28% to 41% on different bases, AJ Bell 32.6% ROIC (flattered by cash interest). Total capital ratio 29% and CET1 22.7%; the binding constraint is the 4.8% leverage ratio, not risk capital. The model can hold return on equity at 38% to 44% through the horizon if the payout stays around 70% to 76%.

C-52

Conversion of net income into free cash flow; working capital and similar

B80

Question asked How high is the conversion of net income into free cash flow, and how do net working capital and similar items behave?

Judgement. Earnings are cash: capital expenditure (fixed and intangible) was SEK 74m in 2025 against net income SEK 2,609m, development is expensed, there is no working capital in the ordinary sense (FactSet shows nil), and dividends of SEK 1,870m were paid from earnings. FactSet's free-cash-flow line (SEK -797m in 2025; Q3 cash flow -SEK 1,929m) is customer deposit and lending flow and must not be used as a conversion measure. The items that behave like working capital for a bank are customer deposits (SEK 111.9bn, moving with risk appetite), the treasury portfolio (SEK 85bn, 84% covered bonds, duration under 0.25 years) and the lending book (SEK 38bn, growing); they determine the leverage ratio, not cash available to shareholders. Conversion for modelling purposes: 95% to 100% of net income after the AT1 coupon (about SEK 60m to 65m a year on SEK 1.3bn at STIBOR plus 2.85% to 3.25%, paid from equity), with the leverage ratio the only constraint on distribution.

C-53

Gross and operating margins, and their trend

B75

Question asked How high are gross and operating profit margins, and how are they trending?

Judgement. Gross margin is not a company measure (FactSet's standardised figure is 77% for 2025; the value-chain research puts platform-stage gross margins near 95%). The operating margin is top-decile and flat: 67% (2024), 68% (2025), 70% (Q1 2026), 70% (Q2), 72% (Q3, seasonal costs), with consensus at 69% to 70% for FY2026 to FY2028. Nordnet reports 70% to 76% depending on basis (its capitalisation flatters it; DNB Carnegie says Avanza's expensing justifies a premium); Fineco runs a 27% cost-to-income ratio; AJ Bell 42% to 43% pre-tax.

The trend to model is not the operating margin but the two ratios beneath it: the revenue margin on savings capital (45 basis points in 2025, 47 in Q1 2026, about 45 in Q2, about 41 in Q3) and the cost-to-savings ratio (14.1 in 2025, about 14 in 2026, 11.7 in Q3 on seasonal costs). The operating margin is their difference and stays flat only if capital grows faster than the revenue margin falls and the cost line grows at 8% to 9%. Morgan Stanley's bear case of 56% to 61% was refuted by 68% to 72%; the realistic downside in a 2022-type year is 60% to 63%; the 2030 cost cap at 5% implies 72% to 75% if revenue grows 8% to 10%.

C-54

Earnings quality: adjusted versus reported, one-offs, capitalised costs, tax and other leakage

B78

Question asked How clean is the earnings base: how large is the gap between adjusted and reported earnings; do one-offs, capitalised costs or other items flatter it; and does the tax rate or any other non-obvious leakage erode earnings per share?

Judgement. Among the cleanest in European financials, with three leakages to carry and one quality caveat. Clean: reported and FactSet-adjusted EPS differ by 0.2%; development and cloud costs are expensed as incurred (the CEO's stated philosophy is to put cash on the cost line); the only acquisition is immaterial; credit losses have been nil since 2011; KPMG unqualified; no performance fees, no capitalised personnel (Nordnet capitalised SEK 223m in 2025). Leakages: (1) tax at 14.5% is structurally low because most of the insurance company's income bears yield tax rather than corporate tax; it was 16% in 2021 and a shift of income mix toward the bank, a change in yield-tax treatment or a bank levy would move it, and 17% would cost about 3% of EPS; (2) AT1 coupons of about SEK 60m to 65m a year are paid from equity, below the EPS line, roughly SEK 0.40 a share or 2% of EPS, and reduce retained capital; (3) warrant dilution, capped at 1.6% across live programmes, about 0.4% a year (466,673 shares issued at SEK 201.90 in September 2025). The caveat: Q3's other income of SEK 69m (41% above consensus) came mainly from Avanza Markets, a line that is not recurring at that level; and 44% of revenue is transactional, so quality is cyclical rather than accounting-driven.

C-75

Fixed-cost operating leverage: how much does revenue move operating profit, and how wide a range?

C60

Question asked How material is the fixed-cost operating leverage: how much does a given change in revenue move operating profit, and how wide a range of outcomes does that create?

Judgement. High and two-sided. The cost base (SEK 1,413m in 2025; about SEK 1,580m in 2026) is almost wholly fixed in the short run (personnel 60%, IT 15%, external services 7%, with transaction-linked costs and payment commissions a small variable element), so a change in revenue drops through at roughly 90%. Arithmetic: on FY2025 figures a 10% revenue change (SEK 450m) moves operating profit by about SEK 400m, or 13% to 14%; the leverage multiplier is 1.3x to 1.5x. Precedent: in 2022 operating income fell about 10% and operating profit about 21% as costs kept growing; in 2025 income rose 15% and profit 17%. Q3 2026 showed the mechanism blunted by a cost beat: income 2% below consensus, profit 2% below. Range of outcomes: the brokers' FY2027 EPS run from SEK 19.3 (DNB Carnegie) to SEK 22.9 (Deutsche Bank), about -8% to +10% around the median, on revenue of SEK 5,440m to 6,054m; the company's own sensitivities (a point of policy rate SEK 420m down or SEK 1.1bn up; 2 points of trading volume about SEK 0.25 a share; 5 points of cross-border mix about SEK 0.35 a share) make a 15% swing in a year's EPS ordinary. The operating leverage is therefore the model's main source of forecast error in both directions, and it is larger on the downside because costs are guided to grow 8% to 9% regardless.

C-76

Financial leverage: amplification of EPS and risk, and comfort

B80

Question asked How material is the financial leverage or gearing: how much does it amplify earnings per share and risk, and how comfortable is it?

Judgement. Conventional financial leverage is nil: no borrowings other than SEK 1.3bn of perpetual AT1 and small leases, net cash SEK 1,489m on FactSet's definition at June 2026, no interest expense of note. It amplifies nothing in EPS. The leverage that matters is regulatory: the non-risk-based leverage ratio (4.8% at September 2026 against a 3.5% requirement including Pillar 2 guidance; 4.2% in March, 4.4% at end-2025), which is the binding constraint because customer deposits inflate the exposure measure (SEK 125bn to 133bn) while risk weights are tiny (risk-weighted exposure SEK 20.9bn; total capital ratio 29%). Comfort: SEK 46bn of deposit capacity before the requirement binds; retained earnings add roughly 60 to 70 basis points of ratio a year at a 70% payout; a severe market crash that drove customers into cash could consume the headroom (exposure toward SEK 168bn). The constraint shows in behaviour already (the mortgage pause; AT1 issued twice; no buyback). For the model: no interest line, the AT1 coupon below EPS, and the leverage ratio as the ceiling on deposit- and lending-driven NII growth and on payout above 70%.

C-77

How much of each major cost part is cuttable?

❌ D45

Question asked How much of each major part of the cost base is cuttable or reducible?

Judgement. Less than the operating-leverage arithmetic implies, because management's practice is to reinvest rather than cut and because the cost base is people and technology that take time to reduce.

  • Personnel (60%, about SEK 910m annualised): 812 employees after +15% in a year; hiring is to slow in 2027; a freeze would hold the line at about 4% salary inflation; a reduction of 5% to 10% of headcount is feasible in a downturn but has no precedent at Avanza (2022 costs still rose).
  • IT and licences (15%, about SEK 235m): the cloud migration (about SEK 50m a year to 2030) is deferrable in timing; licence costs scale with activity; a 10% to 20% cut is possible over a year.
  • External services (7%, about SEK 100m): consultants and the most cuttable line; Q4 2025 saw a temporary uptick for the migration; halving it is feasible.
  • Denmark (SEK 40m in 2026, about SEK 120m in 2027, SEK 140m a year after): entirely discretionary before launch; a deferral would save 2% to 3% of pre-tax profit.
  • Marketing and other (the remainder): small; marketing is largely word of mouth. Realistic cuttable amount in a 2022-type year: 10% to 15% of the cost base over twelve months without damaging the franchise, most of it from Denmark deferral, external services and a hiring freeze. The company has never used it; its response to a downturn has been to hold cost growth, not reverse it.
C-78

Beyond cost cuts: scope to improve outputs over the mid term; peer precedents; few big or many small; congruent?

C62

Question asked Beyond cutting costs, how much plausible scope is there to improve the financial outputs over the mid term; do peer precedents show it is achievable; would it come from a few big areas or many small ones; and is it congruent with the stated strategy?

Judgement. Plausible and congruent, from one or two big levers and many small ones, with peer precedents showing the ceiling is above the current margin. Big levers: (1) the policy rate (each 25 basis points of hike worth SEK 150m to 250m of NII after pass-through, 4% to 7% of pre-tax profit; the forward curve at 2.25% about 4% of 2027 EPS on DNB Carnegie's sum); (2) a recovery of brokerage yield toward 11 basis points and of fund margin toward 23 (together SEK 100m to 150m, 3% to 5% of profit) if activity and mix revert, which DNB Carnegie values at 17% of 2027 EPS in its full-recovery case; (3) the cost cap stepping down to 5% by 2030 (two to four points of margin by then). Small levers: Sigma (SEK 5bn to 10bn by 2028 worth 3% to 5% of fee income), the pension transfer law, Private Banking mortgage tiers (constrained by leverage), automation (K4 forms cut calls 40%), AI development productivity (unquantified), Denmark after 2030. Peer precedents: Nordnet's 71% to 76% margin and Fineco's 27% cost-to-income show the ceiling; flatexDEGIRO's 21% personnel cut shows what a cost reset can do, at the price of a slowing franchise. Congruence: every lever above is one of the five Strategy 2030 priorities or the cost cap. The sum over four years is a path to a 72% to 75% margin on 8% to 10% revenue growth, not a step change.

C-79

Narrowness of the perimeter, its change, and what it means for predictability

C72

Question asked How narrow is the business perimeter (number of countries and business lines), how is it changing, and what does that mean for predictability?

Judgement. Very narrow today, widening from H2 2027, and the perimeter is not the source of unpredictability. One country, one reported segment (Savings and Investments), three regulated entities (Avanza Bank, Avanza Pension, Avanza Fonder), one customer type with three tiers, four revenue lines, one cost number guided. From H2 2027: two countries, two technology platforms, a second regulatory regime and a branch in Copenhagen, with a Danish cost line that is bounded and disclosed. The unpredictability sits inside the narrow perimeter, in the transactional revenue lines and in deposits, both of which the company discloses monthly, so the narrowness converts into trackability rather than into forecast precision. For the Consumer: model Sweden as one segment on four lines and Denmark as a separate cost line of SEK 40m, about SEK 120m and about SEK 140m for 2026 to 2028, with no Danish revenue in the base case before 2029.

Company - Management

C6311 questions
C-5565C-5662C-5762C-57b55C-5858C-5978C-6065C-6165C-6268C-6370C-6450

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

Management is a disciplined, transparent, owner-aligned administrator of a franchise built by others, with a complete and credible team, a congruent culture, outlier standards on service and cost, and a record of delivering what it controls through a difficult rate cycle. It is not a builder yet: the Danish platform, Sigma and the cloud are the tests, and the first six months of each are on budget. Its weaknesses are the ones that matter for a forecast less than for a multiple: no pricing or yield management evident, no dynamic capital allocation, no quantified productivity promise, and a framing of soft quarters that is more reassuring than the numbers. For the Consumer: trust the cost line and the disclosure; do not expect management to lift revenue per unit or to add a buyback leg; and give the Danish and Sigma lines option value only until 2028.

C-55

Ambition and vision, against peers

C65

Question asked How big is the company's ambition or vision (a force: growth as a priority, outcompeting, innovating, adapting, pushing), particularly in relation to peers?

Judgement. The ambition is large in its statement and prudent in its method, and it is now slightly smaller than Nordnet's in both reach and pace. Stated: SEK 2,000bn of savings capital by 2030 (15% a year; from SEK 1,277bn the required rate is now about 12%), "a leading European platform" starting with Denmark, market leadership in Private Banking and in non-collectively-agreed occupational pensions, at least 35% return on equity, a culture of "Better. For real." and "Smarter together". Delivered against it: 13% savings-capital growth in 2025 (short of 15%), 21% in 2026 with market help; the first foreign market in 27 years; a new product category (Sigma) at half the market price. Against peers: Nordnet raised its customer-growth target to 13% to 15%, is launching in Germany (a market 2.4 times the Nordics) a year before Avanza launches in Denmark, and is building a private-banking venture; Fineco targets Germany, France and Spain; AJ Bell doubled its platform in five years. Avanza's vision is bold on scope and cautious on execution risk, which is the operator's character; the market has read it as the smaller ambition and priced it accordingly.

C-56

How great and smart is the strategy: moat widening, growth priority, dynamic across environments?

C62

Question asked How great and smart is the strategy, with right and bold strategic priorities (an enabler): widening the moat over the mid term, growth as a priority, and a dynamic strategy that can capitalise on a plausible range of environments?

Judgement. Smart in each of its parts and sound as a whole; bold enough on ambition and prudent on method; it widens the moat at home and does not address the regional scale gap except through a small, late and dual-platform entry. Smart: internalising deposits at ten times the spread when the regulator forced the change; steering customers to cheaper funds (costing margin, deepening the moat); occupational pensions as a counter-cyclical annuity line; Sigma to hold affluent wallets against banks and Nordnet; a separate Danish platform to protect the Swedish core's release velocity; the cost cap as a public commitment; AI-first development to lower build cost. Right priorities: wallet share over acquisition once penetration passed 20%. Dynamic across environments: asset-light and self-funded, it survives a downturn with margins above 60%; it is not dynamic on pricing, by design. The weaker parts: Denmark is the smaller of the two available prizes with the stronger incumbent; no insurance licence at launch leaves the Danish pension pool to Nordnet; and the dual stack is a permanent cost that a growth strategy would have avoided by reusing the core. The strategy is an extension of the model, not a reinvention, and that is appropriate for a franchise this strong.

C-57

How great is the chief executive, judged by actions?

C62

Question asked How great is the chief executive, judged by actions rather than conjecture (an enabler, not an ossifier)?

Judgement. Very good on the actions that a disciplined administrator produces, unproven on the actions a builder produces, and the next three years test the second. Actions: record operating profit in 2024 (SEK 2,621m), 2025 (SEK 3,078m) and the first nine months of 2026 (SEK 2,727m, +16%); a cost cap formalised in late 2024 and met every year; AT1 priced at STIBOR plus 3.25% then plus 2.85%, beating Nordnet's plus 5.0%; the Sparkonto+ exit executed at 60% retention against a 55% target; the Stabelo stake sold; Sigmastocks bought, integrated and launched within a year; a team rebuilt and a Danish leader recruited; transparent disclosure of the cloud cost, the rate sensitivity and the Danish budget. Against: three forced senior departures in six months of 2025; a former Nordea peer describes a delegative, consensus-seeking style that avoids the hardest conversations; no engineering background and a stated preference not to be first with new technology; no explanation offered for the Q1 2026 yield compression and a "temporary" framing of the Q3 one; a 76% payout while the leverage ratio bound; no buyback. The Wisdom Library's "animal CEO" bar is not met; the "operator quality" bar (direction, resources, record) is met at a solid level. An enabler, not an ossifier; not yet shown to be a builder.

C-57b

Priority on hiring well, and the quality of the team built

C55

Question asked How high a priority is hiring well for the chief executive, and how great is the leadership team the chief executive has built (an enabler)?

Judgement. Hiring is evidently a priority and the team is complete and credible; it was built out of departures rather than succession, it leans on a trusted SEB and Nordea circle, and it has not yet run a dual-platform build. The team: CFO Svärling (21 years at SEB, programmer by training), COO Bonnivier (from Avanza Fonder), CPO Eriksson, CTO Broman, Fonder CEO Wärn (internal), Private Banking head Smith, business development Gjötterberg (an SEB and Nordea colleague of the CEO), Denmark country manager Sillesen (Lunar, Saxo), HR chief Dammert; 11 members, four women. Management says onboarding of critical professionals was much swifter in H1 2026; the Karriärföretagen and Universum rankings (second among technology students, fifth overall) say the employer brand attracts. The cost: a CFO of 13 years, a COO after seven months and an IR head of ten years left in 2025, and the research reads the whole replacement of financial, communications and operational leadership in eighteen months as an outlier against the culture metrics. Retention scaffolding is thin: six-month notice, no multi-year cliff vesting on warrants. The team's quality will be judged by 2028 on Denmark and the cloud; the evidence to date (costs on guide, cloud on budget, Denmark under budget) is favourable and short.

C-58

How well resourced and invested is the whole business?

C58

Question asked How well resourced and invested is the whole business (a buffer; an ant colony: built yesterday for resilience)?

Judgement. Well invested in the customer-facing platform and being re-invested in the back end, with an honest acknowledgement that the back end lagged. For: SEK 300m committed to cloud through 2030 and on schedule; 25 agile teams on one Java stack deploying in minutes; 85%-plus of systems cloud-ready; the Tieto Hub back-office migration done; development expensed, so the invested base is not flattered; headcount +15% in a year; a Danish platform funded; 99.9%-type availability not disclosed but outages self-reported. Against: former employees say development historically favoured front-end features over backend robustness, the Swedish infrastructure was not built for present scale and high-traffic days caused login outages; the CFO himself said internal systems require significant upgrades; the trading core will stay on physical infrastructure; no uptime, incident or recovery-time data is published; Google Cloud is a single source. The ant-colony test (built yesterday for resilience) is passed on the balance sheet (capital ratios, zero credit losses, a matched FX book) and partly passed on technology. The 2026 to 2027 investment years are the buffer being rebuilt, at the cost of operating leverage now.

C-59

How holistic and congruent is the culture?

B78

Question asked How holistic and congruent is the culture, with alignment across the total system (as at Lifco, Avanza or Ryanair)? Such that the goals, strategy, culture, execution, decisions are congruent, holistic and symbiotic?

Judgement. Highly congruent, and the framework's own exemplar: goals (customers first, lower prices, 15% growth, a cost cap), strategy (wallet share, pensions, Private Banking, cost sharing), culture (eNPS 57 against a 50 target; leadership score 86 against 80; staff turnover 9% from 13% in 2023; sickness 2.0%), execution (16 years of top satisfaction, 14 basis points of cost) and decisions (expensing, no bonuses, warrants at market) all point the same way, and the refreshed principles ("Customers first. Always." "Better. For real." "Smarter together.") were launched with a culture week and leadership day in Q3 2026. One dissonance: the 2025 C-suite churn against the posted metrics, and an expert's reading of a cultural shift from customer-centric toward revenue-driven international expansion. One untested edge: whether the congruence survives a second country and a second platform. The culture is a real moat and the reason delivery has been consistent through the leadership rebuild.

C-60

Clarity and helpfulness of communication, including on the driver transmission mechanism

C65

Question asked How clear, straightforward and helpful is the company's communication, including on the strategy and the driver transmission mechanism?

Judgement. Clear and unusually complete on the inputs (the best monthly disclosure in the set), weaker on the economics of the new initiatives and on the framing of a soft quarter. Helpful: monthly statistics on net inflow, customers, savings capital, trading, lending and deposits; quarterly detail on deposit mix, brokerage yield, foreign share, fund margin and income per customer; a published rate sensitivity (SEK 420m down, SEK 1.1bn up per point); the Danish budget phased by year with the capitalisation split; the cloud cost; company-compiled consensus; the cost guide in one number; the 2030 targets in operational terms. Less helpful: no earnings or revenue guidance (a feature of a market-driven business); no Sigma customer count, cost, margin, retention or payback; no quantified productivity or efficiency benefit from cloud or AI; no capital-allocation bridge for the SEK 46bn of leverage headroom; "another strong quarter" for a 2% miss with every revenue line below consensus; "temporary" for a yield compression partly caused by a strategic mix shift; no numbers beyond direction on deposit pass-through. The transmission mechanism (savings capital times margin by line, less a guided cost line, at 14.5% tax) is fully trackable, which is what matters most for the model.

C-61

Record of acting, not talking: recent huge delivery and a mid-term record, beyond guidance and estimates

C65

Question asked How strong is the company's record of acting, not just talking: a recent record of huge delivery and action, and a mid-term track record of delivery, not misses or justifications, whatever the environment (outperforming in tougher ones is even better), beyond its records against guidance and estimates?

Judgement. A strong record of delivering what the company controls (costs, retention, capital, products) through a harder environment, with two misses on what it does not control (growth against target; relative share-price performance) and a first consensus miss on monetisation. Acts: Sparkonto+ wound down on time with 60% retained; SEK 1.3bn of AT1 at falling spreads; Stabelo sold; Sigmastocks bought, integrated and launched; a Danish branch, prototypes, a country manager and an under-budget build before the first SEK 14m of cost; pre-filled K4 forms; the Pension Portal and two B2B partnerships; US extended-hours trading. Mid-term record: operating profit SEK 2,292m (2023), 2,621m (2024), 3,078m (2025), record quarters in Q1 and Q2 2026 and the second-highest in Q3; margins 67% to 72%; all delivered through a rate cycle from 4.0% to 1.75%, which the framework counts as better. Misses: savings capital +13% against a 15% target in 2025; TSR lagging Nordnet by 5% to 8% a year in 2024 and 2025 and by about 22 points over the last twelve months; Q3 2026 operating profit 2% below consensus with income per customer down 6%. Against peers: Nordnet landed costs on guidance four times running too and grew faster; Fineco pulled targets forward. Avanza is an honest tracker, not a serial over-deliverer.

C-62

Outlier standards and a record of out-executing competitors

C68

Question asked To what extent does the company hold outlier standards, driving higher, faster and better, with a record of out-executing competitors?

Judgement. Outlier standards on service, efficiency, credit and accounting; out-execution of competitors on those dimensions and not on growth, where Nordnet now out-executes Avanza. Outliers: 16 consecutive Swedish Quality Index wins; NPS 41 against 3; cost-to-savings 14 basis points against Nordnet's 15 to 16 and banks' multiples of that; zero credit losses since 2011 against 5 to 15 basis points of provisions at typical European digital brokers; a 29% total capital ratio; 20% of Stockholm transactions; 0.7% churn; reported-equals-adjusted earnings. Where competitors out-execute: Nordnet's 50,400 deployments a year, agentic-first product teams, 43% cross-border trades, a Danish pension wrapper, faster customer growth and a 71% Q3 margin on 20% income growth; Fineco's 27% cost-to-income with an adviser network. The question Q3 raises is whether Avanza's standards on monetisation (yield management across fee classes) match its standards on cost; the evidence says not yet.

C-63

Shareholders first, and prudence of governance: what the evidence shows, and why

C70

Question asked Explain, with the evidence, the extent to which the company puts shareholders first, ahead of management, and how prudent its governance is (scaffolding put in place even where it was not needed): what does the evidence show, and why?

Judgement. The evidence shows a company run for shareholders by owner-aligned leadership with conservative pay and prudent scaffolding, with three qualifications: concentrated founder influence and a 29-year chairmanship, equity incentives without performance hurdles, and a payout above policy at a moment when the binding capital ratio was tight. For: one share class, one vote, no shareholder agreements, no voting caps; fixed-only cash pay for the CEO (SEK 9,360k salary in 2024, 74% of total) and CFO, no short-term bonuses anywhere in group management; warrants bought at market on Black-Scholes terms with 10%-of-salary financing, 127,307 held by the CEO, no open-market selling in twelve months, 1.6% dilution cap; board members invest a third of net fees in shares for five years; nine directors independent of management, a Risk, Capital and Audit Committee meeting ten times a year, an IT committee for the cloud transition, three lines of defence, ICAAP-justified distributions, KPMG unqualified since 2019 with a rotating lead partner, related-party dealings at market terms; SEK 1.3bn of AT1 issued ahead of need; development expensed; a 70% payout policy with 76% delivered. Against: only six of nine directors independent of major shareholders; Sven Hagströmer chairman since 1997 with the family and Creades holding 20%; no shareholding requirement for executives (AJ Bell requires 350% of salary); a SEK 15m GDPR fine in 2024 and SEK 29m of customer compensation for an interest-rate error; a 76% payout while AT1 was being issued to hold the leverage ratio. Why the balance is favourable: the scaffolding was put in place before it was needed (AT1 before the deposit wave; the IT committee before the migration), pay cannot reward short-term earnings management, and the founders' interest is the long-run franchise.

C-64

Enlightened capital allocator and dynamic repurchaser, with a high-return allocation record?

C50

Question asked To what extent is the company an enlightened capital allocator and a dynamic repurchaser of its shares, not just an operator or generator, with a record of allocating capital at a high return on new invested capital?

Judgement. An excellent generator and a sound distributor, not a dynamic allocator or repurchaser; the leverage ratio is the reason and the record is short. Record: dividends of SEK 11.50, 11.75 and 12.75 for 2023 to 2025 (70% to 82% payouts); a 10% buyback authorisation renewed and unused; Nordnet, by contrast, bought back SEK 250m in Q1 2026 and SEK 604m in 2025 at a 4.0% to 4.5% leverage target. Enlightened on the small things: AT1 at better spreads than the peer; selling the Stabelo stake while keeping distribution; a SEK 21m technology acquisition that became a product within a year; expensing rather than capitalising. The new allocations are small and unproven: Denmark on a five-year payback, Sigma on no disclosed economics, cloud as a cost of staying in business. The return on reinvestment in the base franchise is above 40%, which is why distributing most of the earnings is correct; the one allocation question the Consumer should carry is whether the SEK 46bn of deposit headroom and the unused buyback are deployed, which management has not addressed.

Risks

C554 questions
C-8055C-8150C-8245C-8368

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

The risk picture has rotated rather than shrunk: the June 2026 clustering around Riksbank cuts has dissolved as the rate cycle turned, and a new clustering has formed around the risk-off regime (trading velocity, risk-appetite-driven deposit swings, market levels) and around monetisation per unit. None of the fundamental risks threatens solvency or the franchise; several threaten a year's earnings by 10% to 25%; the perception risks are live and the stock is already trading as if some have arrived. The multiple is close to a fair trough, which is the main cushion. For the Consumer: run the base case with the transactional lines normalised, carry a 2022-type scenario at 15% to 20% below base EPS for one year, and treat the multiple's further downside as 5% to 15% rather than 25%-plus.

C-80

Has the core growth engine decelerated over the last twelve months?

C55

Question asked To what extent has the core growth engine decelerated over the last twelve months: is the revenue growth rate running below the company's own normal rate, and is the input change force itself slowing?

Judgement. The financial output has not decelerated below the company's normal rate over twelve months (nine-month income +15%, in line with 2024 and 2025 and above the decade's double-digit norm), but the quarterly rate has turned from +26% (Q2) to +12% (Q3), and the input change force behind the transactional half (retail risk appetite expressed as trading velocity) is slowing. The engine behind the recurring half (customers and net inflows) is not slowing. Inputs: customers +8% year on year (52,800 added in Q3; a normal rate for Avanza, below Nordnet's 12%); net inflows SEK 23.1bn in Q3, +61%, and a record fund quarter; savings capital +21%. Velocity: transactions -8% quarter on quarter and -10% year on year in September on Deutsche Bank's tracker (worse than Nordnet's -5% and flatexDEGIRO's -3%); notes -14%; income per customer -6%. DNB Carnegie expects reduced trading velocity after October's market turbulence, and the Researcher's hypothesis of 16 October is that a sell-off cuts client activity rather than lifting it. Read: the engine is intact and the throttle is easing; the next two quarters will show revenue growth in the high single digits to low teens, not the mid-twenties, and the model should not carry a reacceleration without new evidence.

C-81

Sudden, high-magnitude, low-probability fundamental risks: plausibility and size

C50

Question asked What fundamental risks are there that are sudden, high-magnitude and low-probability, and how plausible and how large is each?

Judgement. Six, of which two are large and plausible enough to carry as named scenario lines.

  1. A rate reversal to deep cuts (each point SEK 420m of NII, about 11% of pre-tax profit; a return to 1.0% would be worth about SEK 300m): low probability over eighteen months given a likely November hike; medium beyond, if the risk-off regime becomes a recession. Size: large.
  2. A national inducement rule or bank tax (fund commissions 17% of income, 60% of them Swedish; a bank levy at the sector rate would move the 14.5% tax rate by several points): low to medium probability over three years; the FSA has paused and the political debate is live. Size: 5% to 15% of profit.
  3. A cyber, data or availability failure (a SEK 15m GDPR fine in 2024; documented outages under load; a Google Cloud or TietoEvry single point of failure; Nordnet's SEK 100m naked-shorting sanction shows the supervisor's scale): medium plausibility; size depends on trust damage rather than on the fine; the risks report names platform reliability and cyber as the strongest bear evidence.
  4. An equity crash with a dash to cash (transaction income falls 20%-plus while deposits surge and consume leverage headroom; a 2022-type year took EPS down 19%): low probability in any given year, medium over four; size: 15% to 25% of EPS for a year.
  5. A Danish price war on foreign exchange (up to SEK 200m across both platforms if fees fall below 25 basis points): low probability before 2028; size moderate.
  6. Agentic cash sweeping or a Swedish property shock lifting loan-to-value ratios: tail, untested, small within the horizon. The compound risk the June risks report flagged, that these cluster around a single rate-cut axis, has eased because the axis has turned; the new clustering is around the risk-off regime (velocity, deposits, markets) and it hits earnings, not solvency.
C-82

Sudden, high-magnitude perception and sentiment risks, including read-across

❌ D45

Question asked What perception- or sentiment-driven risks are there that are sudden and high-magnitude, including events elsewhere in the sector, theme or market that could hit the share price whatever the company's own fundamentals?

Judgement. Read-across shocks are the ordinary condition of this sector and are live now: the share has already fallen 7.3% over three months and 5% since the Q3 print, is at its 200-day average, carries a technical momentum pin of D and a relative-strength percentile of 38. The named triggers: Nordnet's Q3 on 22 October (a strong German-launch narrative widens the growth gap in the market's eyes); a Riksbank surprise on 4 November in either direction (a hold would remove the one earnings tailwind, a hike could be read as a risk-appetite negative); a US retail-trading or AI-trade reversal (foreign trading is 31% of turnover and 70% US; the FfF regime has the first credit crack inside the AI chain); a Swedish equity drawdown; further sector downgrades (flatexDEGIRO downgraded by Deutsche Bank in October; IG Group -45% and Plus500 -22% over three months show how the leveraged end sells off); and a sector-wide re-pricing on rates if the hiking cycle is read as demand-destructive. Size and liquidity are not the issue (USD 15m to 18m a day traded; SEK 61bn market capitalisation; local long-only and founder holders). Mitigant: the multiple is already at the 12th percentile of its own decade, which limits the perception downside but has not prevented a 12% fall from the 52-week high.

C-83

How far could the shares fall to a fair trough multiple?

C68

Judgement. A small distance on the multiple alone, a larger one if earnings are cut with it. The stock is at 16.2x current consensus (12th percentile of ten years) and 16.7x 24-month-forward consensus of about SEK 22.5 (19th percentile of five years, 5th of two years; the ten-year "crash" band is 15.9x and the five-year low 13.0x; the sector median is 15.2x). On unchanged earnings: to 15.9x gives about SEK 358 (-1%); to the sector median 15.2x about SEK 342 (-5%); to the five-year low 13.0x about SEK 293 (-19%). With 24-month-forward EPS cut 10% to about SEK 20.3 (a 2022-style normalisation): 15.9x gives SEK 323 (-10%), 15.2x gives SEK 309 (-14%), 13.0x gives SEK 264 (-27%). The realistic trough on a fair multiple is therefore SEK 310 to 345, 4% to 14% below SEK 360, which is also the memo's own "bullish below SEK 360" level and close to the 52-week low of SEK 307. The memo's exit trigger (a sequential operating margin below 50%) remains far away (72% in Q3).

Back to contents

Guidance

C576 questions
C-6850C-7055C-7158C-7262C-8552C-7365

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

Guidance at Avanza is a cost ceiling, a capital frame and a set of 2030 targets, all specific, all set to be met, and none of them an earnings number. The result is that the sell-side's revenue line is unanchored by the company and anchored instead to markets, the Riksbank and management's qualitative tone, which after Q3 is more confident than the run rate. For the Consumer: take the cost guide as the base (SEK 1,580m for 2026, SEK 1,800m to 1,850m for 2027 with Denmark), expect it to be beaten by 1% to 2%, and treat consensus revenue for FY2026 as 1% to 2% high after Q3 and FY2027 revenue as dependent on a velocity recovery and a 2.25% STIBOR path that are possible but not in the company's commentary.

C-68

Company guidance versus sell-side consensus for this year and next, and why

C50

Question asked To what extent is company guidance above or below sell-side consensus for this financial year and next financial year, and why?

Judgement. Avanza gives no earnings or revenue guidance; the only guidance is on costs, and consensus has it in line for 2026 and slightly light for 2027. For FY2026: Swedish cost growth 9% on SEK 1,413m gives about SEK 1,540m plus SEK 40m for Denmark, about SEK 1,580m; consensus (13 October) implies SEK 1,596m (revenue 5,225 less EBIT 3,629), in line. Nine-month costs were SEK 1,145m, so Q4 at about SEK 435m to 440m is implied, consistent with Q4 seasonality (Q4 2025 was SEK 402m, +20%). For FY2027: the 8% framework on about SEK 1,540m gives about SEK 1,665m plus Denmark (SEK 80m running from launch plus marketing of up to SEK 60m, partly in H2 2027, plus the remaining pre-launch budget of SEK 80m to 110m) gives roughly SEK 1,800m to 1,850m; consensus implies SEK 1,748m (5,726 less 3,978), so consensus costs look SEK 50m to 100m light, or 1% to 2% of EBIT. Why: the cost guide is set to be met (four years of beats) while consensus seems to under-model the Danish phasing, which is back-loaded and only partly in brokers' models (DNB Carnegie's 3% EPS trim for Denmark is the exception). On revenue there is no guidance; the gap between consensus and the company's qualitative commentary is addressed at C-85.

C-70

The record, nature and behaviour of the company and the CEO in upgrading guidance

C55

Question asked Beyond meeting or beating, what is the record, nature and behaviour of the company and its chief executive in upgrading guidance?

Judgement. Avanza does not upgrade guidance; it sets a cost ceiling and lands under it. The pattern is sandbagging on costs and silence on revenue: 2022 costs 2.7% under the guided floor, 2023 0.7% under, 2024 9.2% against 9.5% guided, 2025 10.4% against 11%, 2026 Danish costs cut from SEK 50m to 40m while the Swedish 9% was held. The CEO's stated practice is to guide only when highly certain and to put cash on the cost line. Long-term targets were set once (October 2024) and never moved; the 15% savings-capital target was missed in 2025 without comment beyond the figure. For the Consumer: the only guidance-driven upgrade impulse available is a cost beat of 0.5% to 2% of the cost line (SEK 10m to 30m, under 1% of profit); revenue upgrades come from markets and the Riksbank, not from management. The quality of this behaviour is high (honest tracker); its utility for an upgrade cycle is low.

C-71

How conservative is current guidance for the next one to three years against the last one to five, adjusted for change forces?

C58

Question asked How conservative is current guidance for the next one to three years against the last one to five years, adjusted for the difference in input change forces versus the past?

Judgement. Conservative on costs, ambitious on growth, and the two are set against different histories. Costs: the 8% average to 2030 (9% in 2026, 5% by 2030) compares with about 11% a year over 2022 to 2025 (SEK 1,031m to 1,413m) and 15% in Q2 2026; it is therefore below the recent run rate and requires the hiring slowdown management has signalled for 2027 and the end of cloud spend by 2030. Adjusted for change forces (Denmark excluded from the 8%, the cloud inside it), the Swedish guide is achievable and the group total will run 2 to 3 points above it until 2028. Growth: 15% a year savings capital against 13% in 2025 and 21% now (with markets); the required rate from here is 12%, which a 7% market plus 5% net inflow delivers in a normal year and a flat market does not. Return on equity at least 35% against 40% to 44% delivered: conservative. Payout 70% against 76% to 82% delivered: conservative. Net: the guidance that exists is set to be beaten modestly on costs and returns and to be missed in a weak-market year on growth; it does not contain any earnings cushion because it contains no earnings number.

C-72

How specific is the guidance: building blocks, or vague?

C62

Question asked How specific is the guidance: built from stated building blocks, or vague about how results come about?

Judgement. Specific where it exists and absent where the market most needs it. Building blocks given: Swedish cost growth as a percentage; Danish cost by year in kronor with the capitalisation split (20%), the running cost (SEK 80m) and the marketing cap (SEK 60m for three years); the cloud budget (SEK 300m to 2030); the rate sensitivity table (SEK 420m down, SEK 1.1bn up per point); the leverage-ratio headroom (SEK 46bn); the payout policy and its conditions; the 2030 targets in operational terms; monthly disclosure of every volume driver. Not given: any revenue, income-per-customer, revenue-margin, NII or EPS guidance; any Sigma or pension economics; any deposit pass-through number; any productivity target. The result is a transmission mechanism that is fully specified on the cost and capital side and left to the analyst on the revenue side, which is appropriate for a market-driven business and is why forecast dispersion is wide (FY2027 EPS SEK 19.3 to 22.9).

C-85

Informal and qualitative commentary against formal guidance and consensus, and why

C52

Question asked To what extent is informal/qualitative company commentary above or below formal guidance and sell-side consensus for this financial year and next financial year, and why?

Judgement. Management's qualitative commentary is above consensus on structure and below the pre-Q3 consensus on near-term velocity, and it is framed more positively than the numbers. Above: "another strong quarter", "a gradual economic turnaround", customers moving savings from deposits into funds and shares, hiring to slow in 2027, Denmark on plan and under budget, Sigma a successful launch, higher rates would improve the net interest margin. Below: income declined from Q2 because lower volatility reduced trading and lower deposits reduced liquidity income; the yield fall is "temporary market sentiment" rather than mix; efficiency gains are being reinvested, not banked; new mortgage applications for SEK 3m to 10m customers paused. The sell-side reads the same words two ways: DNB Carnegie (Buy, SEK 425) treats the miss as cyclical and keeps a conservative base with 17% upside if H1 activity returns and 4% more on rates; Deutsche Bank (Buy, SEK 440) keeps a 2027 estimate about 10% above consensus on the asset engine; Jefferies (Underperform, SEK 335) reads structurally lower income per customer. The pre-Q3 consensus FY2026 revenue of SEK 5,225m needs a Q4 of about SEK 1,350m, 5% above Q3, which the commentary on lower velocity does not support unless November's hike and a Q4 activity recovery both arrive; the informal commentary therefore sits below the FY2026 consensus by 1% to 2% on revenue and in line with it on costs.

C-73

What else is material or uncommon about guidance or investor relations?

C65

Question asked What else is material or uncommon about the company's guidance or investor relations?

Judgement. Five things.

  1. The monthly statistics (net inflow, customers, savings capital, trading, lending, deposits) are the most useful disclosure in the comparison set and make the case trackable at a frequency most companies do not allow.
  2. The company compiles and publishes its own consensus before each quarter (18 contributors for Q3), which removes ambiguity about what "the bar" was.
  3. The expensing philosophy (cloud, development, Danish build at 80%) means costs hit the income statement as incurred and there is no balance-sheet signal to watch for overruns; the cost line itself is the signal.
  4. The rate sensitivity is published and updated each quarter (SEK 450m in 2025, SEK 420m in 2026), which is uncommon and is the single most-used number by the sell-side.
  5. Two gaps uncommon for a company this transparent: no uptime or incident statistics despite outages being an expert-flagged risk, and no Sigma metrics beyond assets. The AT1 prospectus (April 2026) is a useful secondary source on deposit tiering and sensitivities.

Sell-side estimates

C523 questions
C-6758C-6958C-7435

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

The sell-side's model is directionally right (a deceleration from mid-teens to high single digits), slightly high for FY2026 after Q3, and reliant for FY2027 to FY2028 on a rate hike and a stabilisation of yield that are plausible but not yet evidenced; it has not been cut, so the revision risk is to the downside by a few percent in the next round and the stock's revision-momentum rating of A is a lagging reading. The company's record says it beats by 2% to 5% in active quarters and misses by 2% to 5% in quiet ones, and Q4 2026 and Q1 2027 begin in quiet conditions. For the Consumer: build the base case 1% to 3% below the 13 October consensus on FY2026 and FY2027 EPS (about SEK 19.0 and 20.3), with the asset lines at or above consensus and the transactional lines below, and keep DNB Carnegie's full-recovery case (+17%) and the rate case (+4%) as explicit upside scenarios.

C-67

Sell-side forecasts for the next one to three years against the last one to five, by line and by granular driver, and how credible the gap is

C58

Question asked How do the sell-side's forecasts for the next one to three years compare with the last one to five years, for the main financial statement line items in particular and the granular working up to them, and how credible is the gap given the change forces and foundations found above?

Judgement. Consensus (FactSet, 13 October, pre-Q3; 11 to 13 brokers) forecasts a deceleration from mid-teens to high single digits that is credible in direction, modestly high in level for FY2026 and dependent on two recoverable but unproven inputs for FY2027 and FY2028. The lines. Revenue SEK 5,225m (FY2026, +16% on the company's SEK 4,495m), 5,726m (+9.6%), 6,216m (+8.6%), 6,616m (FY2029, one broker). EBIT SEK 3,629m (69.5% margin), 3,978m (69.5%), 4,274m (68.8%). EPS SEK 19.40, 20.87, 22.88, 24.60; DPS SEK 14.52, 15.41, 16.93; capital expenditure SEK 63m to 90m. By line for FY2026 to FY2028: NII SEK 1,877m, 2,197m, 2,417m; brokerage SEK 1,346m, 1,394m, 1,503m; fund commissions SEK 946m, 1,109m, 1,239m; currency SEK 825m, 839m, 881m; other SEK 220m, 228m, 246m. Against history. Company-basis income grew 25% a year 2019 to 2025 and 15% a year 2022 to 2025; EPS grew 33% a year 2019 to 2025 and 5.9% a year 2021 to 2025; operating margin 67% to 68% in 2024 and 2025 against 56% to 64% in 2022 to 2023 and 59% to 64% in 2020 to 2021. Consensus three-year EPS growth of 11.4% a year is below the long run and above the post-boom run; the margin at 69% to 70% is at the top of history. The granular checks. (1) FY2026 revenue needs Q4 of about SEK 1,350m against Q3's SEK 1,283m; the nine-month run by line (NII 1,368, brokerage 1,011, fund 680, currency 623) means Q4 needs NII about 510, brokerage about 335, fund about 265, currency about 200, which requires both a rate hike effect that cannot arrive before December and a brokerage rebound; FY2026 consensus is therefore 1% to 2% high and post-Q3 cuts (DNB Carnegie to SEK 18.90) are the start of that. (2) FY2027 revenue +9.6% at a revenue margin of about 43 basis points implies average savings capital of about SEK 1,330bn, 4% above September's level; that is conservative if markets hold and net inflows run 5% to 8%, and it is the source of DNB Carnegie's "17% upside if H1 activity returns". (3) FY2027 NII of SEK 2,197m (+17%) requires the forward curve (STIBOR 2.25%) and stable deposits of about SEK 115bn; plausible after a November hike, exposed if risk-on keeps draining deposits (8.9% of savings capital in Q3, from 9.6%). (4) Brokerage flat to +4% a year and currency +2% a year are sensible normalisation assumptions after the H1 2026 peak. (5) Costs at 9.5% growth in FY2027 are 1% to 2% light on the Danish phasing. (6) The margin held at 69% to 70% through a Danish build is the least conservative element; 68% to 69% is more likely. Credibility. The gap to history is a deceleration, which the change forces support; the level for FY2026 is slightly high; the FY2027 to FY2028 path is credible on volumes and leans on a rate hike and a yield stabilisation at about 10.5 basis points that Q3 did not yet show. Dispersion is wide (FY2027 EPS SEK 19.34 to 22.85), with the bulls (Deutsche Bank, Nordea, Pareto) on the asset engine and the bears (Jefferies, a restricted Sell at SEK 360) on income per customer.

C-69

Record of missing, meeting or beating guidance and estimates over one, three and five years, in comparable conditions

C58

Question asked What is the company's record of missing, meeting or beating guidance and sell-side estimates over the last one, three and five years, including in comparable environmental conditions?

Judgement. A record of small beats in active quarters and small misses in quiet or de-risking quarters, symmetric with the trading cycle and averaging a modest beat; a record of always meeting or beating the cost guide; and a record of missing its own growth target once. Last year: Q4 2025 in line (operating profit SEK 733m; strong cost control noted), Q1 2026 in line (SEK 879m against 877m), Q2 2026 +4% on operating profit and +4.4% on EPS, Q3 2026 -2% on operating profit and -2% on EPS with every revenue line below consensus. Last three years: Q1 2024 +2% (costs 4% better), Q2 2024 a miss (GDPR fine and compensation), Q4 2024 +2%, Q1 2025 +4% (a trading pickup), Q2 2025 -2% on EPS (de-risking into cash), Q3 2025 +5% (cost control). Five years: FY2022 was the one large miss, when EPS fell 19% after the 2021 boom and the sell-side had extrapolated. In comparable conditions (falling or stable volatility after a strong run) the company tends to miss by 2% to 5%; in rising-volatility conditions it beats by 4% to 5%. Cost guidance: beaten in 2022, 2023, 2024 and 2025. Targets: savings capital 13% against 15% in 2025. Against peers: Nordnet's Q2 2026 pre-tax beat was 10% and Q1 2026 a 3% to 5% miss; flatexDEGIRO beat by 14% in Q2 2026; the pattern of trading-driven symmetry is sector-wide.

C-74

Have estimates been cut deeply and fully, and has guidance been rebased lower or kitchen-sinked?

❌ D35

Question asked To what extent have sell-side estimates been cut deeply and fully across all analysts, and has company guidance recently been rebased lower, or more aggressively kitchen-sinked or sandbagged?

Judgement. No on all three counts, which is the opposite of a trough setup and is a risk to carry: estimates sit near their twelve-month highs, guidance has not been rebased (the one change was favourable), and the first post-Q3 revisions are small trims rather than resets. Evidence: consensus FY2027 EPS is up 13% over twelve months, 8% over six, 2% over three and 1% over one (FactSet, 22 October, on the FY2027 series); sales estimates up 11%, 5%, 1%, 1%; the target price up 6% over six months and flat over three; the buy share rose to 50% (+3 points over three months); the dashboard's revision-momentum score is 10 and the pinned sell-side momentum rating is A. The series mostly predates the 16 October print; after it DNB Carnegie cut FY2026 EPS to SEK 18.90 from 19.04, held FY2027 at 19.29, cut FY2028 to 20.79 and trimmed its target to SEK 425; Deutsche Bank held SEK 440; Jefferies held SEK 335. On guidance, the Danish 2026 cost was lowered, the 9% Swedish guide held, nothing sandbagged beyond the habitual cost cushion. For the Consumer this means the estimate cycle is at or past its peak for FY2026, FY2027 estimates have a 1% to 3% downside bias in the next revision round unless the Riksbank hikes, and no "estimates rebased" cushion exists in the price; the cushion is the multiple.

Stock market narrative

C581 question
C-2258

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

The narrative today is the one the Q3 print wrote: a franchise whose asset growth is not converting into earnings at the rate the market assumed, inside a sector priced as a rate and risk-off loser. The staircase that could change it has two novel steps, a November rate hike (near, probable, modest) and the Danish launch (a year away, unpriced, binary), and a run of known steps (cost delivery, the 2027 guide, monthly flows) that confirm quality rather than create news. The market is not paying for quality in this sector; it is paying for growth, and Avanza's growth narrative does not have a new chapter until H2 2027. For the Consumer: the model should not assume a re-rating before the Danish disclosures of H1 2027, and the earnings narrative the market will test over the next two quarters is yield recovery, not asset growth.

C-22

The staircase of novel positive developments: next six months, months six to eighteen, months eighteen to thirty-six; novelty and foundation

C58

Question asked What staircase of novel positive developments lies ahead: which new, dated things can the market look forward to within the next 6 months, in months 6 to 18, and in months 18 to 36; how novel is each; and how well-founded?

Judgement. There is a dated staircase, most of whose steps are known to the market and two of which are genuinely novel and unpriced: the Danish launch and the turn in the rate cycle. The staircase is well founded on company-controlled events and thinly founded on the two market-driven ones that would move estimates most.

Next six months (to April 2027).

  • Nordnet's Q3 on 22 October 2026: a read-across, not Avanza's step; novelty nil; it sets the relative-growth narrative into year-end.
  • The Riksbank's decision on 4 November 2026: a hike to 2% is the one dated, novel, estimate-moving positive within six months (worth 4% to 7% of pre-tax profit annualised, from Q1 2027); well founded on Barclays' and Deutsche Bank's reads, not in the company's commentary.
  • Monthly statistics for October to December: the test of whether velocity recovers; known, not novel.
  • The Retail Investment Strategy final vote (November 2026): confirmation of no inducement ban; largely priced.
  • The preliminary FY2026 statement and dividend proposal on 22 January 2027 (consensus DPS about SEK 14.5, a 4% yield), with the 2027 cost guide: the sector digest's own test of whether the expansion bill is absorbed inside the old cost growth rate; moderately novel; well founded on four years of delivery.
  • Danish pre-launch disclosures in H1 2027 (fee schedule, wrapper readiness, licence): novel and currently unknown; the first information on whether Avanza undercuts Nordnet's 25 basis point foreign-exchange fee.
  • Sigma assets at year-end: novel, small.

  • The Q1 2027 report on 16 April 2027: the second test of yield recovery. Months six to eighteen (April 2027 to April 2028).

  • The April 2027 AGM: a buyback authorisation that could, for the first time, be used if deposits shrink; not mooted.

  • The Danish launch in H2 2027 with first customer and asset numbers: the single most novel and least priced step on the staircase (the sector digest: the price does not pay for Denmark); well founded on a budget, a branch, a leader and prototypes; thinly founded on economics.
  • The Ministry of Finance's answer on pension transfers: novel, binary, unpriced, uncertain in timing.
  • The 2027 STIBOR path against 2.25%: market-driven.
  • Nordnet's German cohort data: read-across for the export theme.

  • Cloud migration milestones and a possible second AT1: known. Months eighteen to thirty-six (April 2028 to October 2029).

  • Danish cohort data through 2028 (mid-2028 is when acquisition must be visibly accelerating): novel, binary.

  • Cost growth stepping toward 5% and the first visible operating leverage from the 2026 to 2027 investment years: known, well founded.
  • Sigma at a size that matters (SEK 5bn to 10bn): novel if it happens.
  • A second European market under the 2030 mandate: novel, unannounced.
  • The 2030 target review at the next capital markets day: known. Novelty is concentrated in Denmark and the rate turn; foundation is strongest on costs and weakest on yield recovery. The staircase supports a narrative that improves from H2 2027, not before.

The Investment Analyst's closing notes for this layer

Closing note for the Consumer: the ten things the model must get right

  1. Savings capital: SEK 1,277bn at September 2026, growing on market return plus 5% to 8% net inflow; 10% to 12% a year in the base.
  2. Revenue margin: about 45 basis points in FY2025, about 41 in Q3 2026 (seasonally low); carry 43 to 44 for 2027 drifting 1 basis point a year, with brokerage at 10.3 to 10.8 basis points of turnover, fund margin 22 basis points, currency 25 basis points on 30% foreign turnover, and NII driven by deposits of SEK 110bn to 120bn and the policy rate.
  3. The policy rate: 2.0% from November 2026 in the base; SEK 420m per point down, SEK 1.1bn per point up, at 50% to 80% pass-through.
  4. Costs: SEK 1,580m (2026), SEK 1,800m to 1,850m (2027), 8% thereafter on the Swedish base, plus Denmark SEK 140m a year from 2028; beaten by 1% to 2%.
  5. Operating margin: 68% to 70% through 2028, rising only as the cost cap tightens.
  6. Below the line: tax 14.5% (17% in the adverse case), AT1 coupons of about SEK 60m from equity, share count +0.4% a year, payout 70% to 76%.
  7. Denmark: a cost line only to 2029; option value after.
  8. Base-case EPS: about SEK 19.0 (FY2026), 20.3 (FY2027), 22.0 to 22.5 (FY2028), with a 2022-type year at 15% to 20% below.
  9. The multiple: 16x to 17x today, a fair range of 17x to 20x, a trough of 15x to 16x.
  10. The two inputs on which the forecast error concentrates: turnover per customer (and its foreign share) and the deposit base's response to risk appetite.
Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for Layer 2

Research requests for the next set

Company: Avanza Bank Holding AB (AZA-SE), Sweden's largest digital savings and investment platform, serving 2.39 million retail, Private Banking and Pro customers with SEK 1,277bn of savings capital through one reported segment (Savings and Investments) and three regulated entities (Avanza Bank, Avanza Pension, Avanza Fonder); income in FY2025 of SEK 4,495m split net interest income 33%, net brokerage 28%, fund commissions 17%, currency-related income 16% and other 6%; organic entry into Denmark planned for the second half of 2027.

Request: T-P1

Peers: Nordnet (SAVE-SE), the direct pan-Nordic peer on a single platform across four countries, launching in Germany in November 2026 and the benchmark for cost-to-savings-capital and for the economics of a greenfield entry; FinecoBank (FBK-IT), the European benchmark for a sub-30% cost-to-income ratio and for guiding operating-cost growth down over a multi-year plan while entering Germany; AJ Bell (AJB-GB), a UK platform that grew distribution spend 41% while holding a 40%-plus margin, the precedent for marketing-led growth against a fixed cost base; flatexDEGIRO (FTK-DE), the precedent for a large personnel cost reset (a 21% reduction in 2026) and for a cost base that absorbed a doubling of customer acquisition cost; Hargreaves Lansdown (private since 2025), the precedent for legacy technology remediation spend and a software impairment at a scaled platform; Swissquote (SQN-CH), a single-core platform with multi-market expansion costs. Inputs: What is the split of Avanza's cost base between fixed and variable, line by line (personnel about 60%, IT systems and licences about 15%, external services about 7%, payment and other commissions, marketing), and which lines move with trading volume, with savings capital, with customer numbers and with none of them? How did operating costs and the operating margin behave in Avanza's 2022 downturn (operating income fell about 10% while costs rose) and in the 2020 to 2021 boom, and how did Nordnet's and flatexDEGIRO's behave in the same periods? What did Avanza's cost guidance promise and deliver each year from 2019 to 2025 (the company reports 9.2% delivered against 9.5% guided in 2024 and 10.4% against 11% in 2025) and what has driven the beats? What is the evidence on the phasing and cost of the Google Cloud migration (the company has guided SEK 300m through 2030, expensed) and on cloud hosting costs exceeding on-premise costs at comparable platforms? How much of the Danish build (the company guides SEK 120m to 150m pre-launch with 20% capitalised, about SEK 80m a year of running cost and up to SEK 60m a year of marketing for three years) is fixed, and what have comparable greenfield entries (Nordnet in Germany, Fineco in the UK to 2023 and in Germany from 2026, Nordnet's earlier German exit in 2010) actually cost against their budgets and over what period? What is the evidence that AI-assisted software development has reduced platform build or maintenance cost at any listed platform, with figures? Which cost lines have proved cuttable in a downturn at European digital platforms and which have not, and how long did reductions take to reach the income statement?
