Where the change comes from, in order of size. (1) The removal of the three revenue tailwinds that made 2023 to 2026 exceptional (the 2022 to 2023 rate cycle, the 2025 to 2026 deposit internalisation, the H1 2026 activity peak): together they explain about half of the deceleration from 15% to 8%. (2) The revenue-margin drift of about 1.2 basis points a year (price and mix, P-04 and P-05): about 3 points of revenue growth a year. (3) The Danish bill (SEK 130m to 180m a year from 2027): about 3 points of cost growth in 2027 and 1 point of operating margin through 2029. (4) The cost escalator at 8% stepping to 5%: neutral to 2029, a 1-point margin gain in 2030. (5) Below the line: a steady 2.5% to 3% a year off EPS growth relative to operating profit growth (P-08). (6) Offsets: the November 2026 hike (about 3% of 2027 pre-tax profit), lending growth (about 2% a year), the scale loop on the cost ratio.

What does not change. The quality: no capital need, earnings that are cash, the cleanest accounting in the set, zero credit losses, a 14.5% tax rate, a 4% dividend yield growing with earnings, churn under 1%, and a volume engine (customers, capital, flows) that the four reports and the first set agree is the most predictable thing in the model. The regime change is in growth, not in robustness. That distinction matters for the fourth set: the market has priced the stock (16.2x, 12th percentile of its ten-year range) as if the robustness were in doubt, while consensus has priced the earnings as if the growth regime had not changed. Both cannot be right, and the base case says the second is the one that adjusts.

The skew and the width of the cone, as inputs for the third set. For the next 12 months the skew is down on revenue (velocity has turned, the regime is risk-off, consensus has not been cut) and up on NII (a hike is likely and a second possible), and the net skew on EPS is mildly down (my base 2% to 4% below consensus with a bear case 15% to 20% below and a bull case 10% to 12% above). The cone over 12 months is about plus or minus 10% on EPS around my base at one standard deviation, set by the operating leverage on the transactional 44% of revenue; over four years it widens to about plus or minus 20%, with the policy rate (plus or minus 1 point is plus or minus 11% to 25% of pre-tax profit) and a 2022-type activity year as the two drivers that could take an outcome outside it. The drivers of the difference between the past and the forecast are concentrated: three inputs (turnover per customer and its foreign share, the deposit base's response to risk appetite and rates, and the fund and brokerage yields) explain more than 80% of the variance, and all three are disclosed monthly or quarterly, which is what makes the case trackable even though it is not precisely forecastable.

P-10

The carefully considered base case for the key operational drivers and the key financial line items over the next 18 months to four years

C54

Question asked Pulling together all the above answers, in all sets so far, what is your carefully considered base case for the key operational drivers and the key financial line items over the next 18 months to four years?

Judgement. The base case is a franchise compounding its assets at 10% to 12% a year, converting them into revenue at a falling yield so that revenue grows 7.5% to 8.5%, carrying a cost line that grows 13% in 2027 (Denmark) and then decelerates to 4% by 2030, and delivering EPS of about SEK 19.1 (FY2026), 20.2 (FY2027), 21.7 (FY2028), 23.3 (FY2029) and 25.3 (FY2030): growth of 15%, 6%, 7%, 8% and 9%, a compound rate of 8.8% a year from the FY2025 base of SEK 16.57, against consensus of SEK 19.40, 20.87 and 22.88 for the first three years (an 11.4% compound rate). The assumptions behind it are deliberately central rather than cautious: a policy rate of 2.0% from November 2026 with STIBOR at 2.25% in 2027 (one hike, not two), equity markets returning 5% a year from a flat Q4 2026, net inflows of 6% of opening capital, trading velocity at a normal (not H1 2026) level, the cost guide met with a 1% beat, Denmark on its disclosed budget and phasing, tax at 14.5%, a 75% payout, no buyback. It is 2% to 5% below consensus because of two things only: the revenue margin (I carry about 42 basis points for 2027 against the roughly 43 to 43.5 that consensus revenue implies) and the Danish cost phasing (about SEK 50m to 100m heavier than consensus implies in 2027). Everything else is in line.

The operational drivers.

Base case against consensus -1.5% -3% -5% -5%

The FY2025 line split is the company's stated 2025 mix (33%, 28%, 17%, 16%, 6%) applied to SEK 4,495m and is approximate to within SEK 20m a line.

The next 18 months by quarter. Q3 2026 actual: income SEK 1,283m (+12.3%), costs 363, operating profit 920 (71.7% margin, seasonally light costs), EPS 4.90. Q4 2026: income about SEK 1,290m (NII 480 with two months of the hike and lending growth; brokerage 310; fund 250; currency 192; other 58), costs about 430 (Q4 is the seasonal cost peak: SEK 402m in Q4 2025), operating profit about 860 (66.7%), EPS about 4.55; full year SEK 19.1. 2027 by quarter: income about 1,390, 1,395, 1,390, 1,425 (growth +10.6%, +4.6%, +8.3%, +10.5%, the Q2 figure low because Q2 2026 was the activity peak); costs about 430, 445, 425, 480 (the Danish launch and marketing in H2); operating profit about 960, 950, 965, 945; margins 69.1%, 68.1%, 69.4%, 66.3%; EPS about 5.1, 5.0, 5.1, 5.0. The quarterly shape matters for the fourth set: Q1 2027 (reported 16 April 2027) is the first quarter with a full rate-hike effect and an easy comparison on costs, and the likeliest beat; Q2 2027 (July 2027) faces the Q2 2026 peak and is the likeliest miss; Q4 2027 carries the Danish launch costs and the lowest margin of the horizon.

What the base case assumes about the three concentrated inputs. (1) Velocity: turnover per customer in 2027 at about 90% of the 2026 average (which included the H1 peak), rising 2% to 3% a year thereafter with the foreign share stable at 31% to 32%; this puts brokerage plus currency at SEK 2,160m in 2027 against SEK 2,140m in 2026 and SEK 1,978m in 2025. (2) Deposits and rates: deposits at about 8.5% to 9% of savings capital, growing with customers; one 25 basis point hike passed through at 60% to 70%; NII +15% in 2027 (DNB Carnegie +11% at SEK 2,042m; consensus +17% at SEK 2,197m). (3) Yields: as in the driver table, two-thirds of the Q3 2026 compression persisting.

The scenarios the Consumer should carry beside the base.

  • Upside (probability about 25%): "H1 2026 returns, and the curve is right." Velocity back to the H1 2026 level in 2027 (brokerage plus currency SEK 2,450m, +SEK 290m), a second hike to 2.25% in H1 2027 (+SEK 100m of 2027 NII), deposits held by risk-off at 9.5% of capital (+SEK 50m): operating profit 2027 about SEK 4,250m, EPS about SEK 22.5 (+11% on base, 8% above consensus). This is DNB Carnegie's 17% full-recovery case plus its 4% rate case, scaled to my base. It requires the activity cycle to turn up again inside twelve months of turning down, which the 2022 to 2023 precedent (two years of lower activity) argues against.
  • Downside (probability about 25%): "a 2022-type year in 2027." Revenue minus 6% to 8% on 2026 (brokerage plus currency down 25% to SEK 1,600m, fund capital flat on a 15% market fall, deposits up to 11% of capital adding SEK 100m of NII as the partial cushion): revenue about SEK 4,800m, costs still SEK 1,750m (no cut; a small Danish slip), operating profit about SEK 3,050m, EPS about SEK 16.2 (minus 20% on base, minus 15% year on year, and 22% below consensus). The leverage ratio would tighten, the payout would hold at 70%, and the multiple would be tested at 15x to 16x on the lower earnings (the SEK 260 to 300 region). This is the case the first set's risk group sized at 15% to 25% of EPS and it remains the governing risk.
  • Base (probability about 50%) as above. The distribution is roughly symmetric around the base in magnitude (plus 11%, minus 20%) and slightly skewed down in probability-weighted terms for 2027 (expected EPS about SEK 19.9 against a base of SEK 20.2), which is the quantitative form of the "lean down" in P-06.

What would move me off the base, and when I would know. Up: a Riksbank hike on 4 November followed by a second by April 2027; October to December monthly statistics showing trades per customer back above the Q2 2026 level; Q4 brokerage above SEK 330m; income per customer above SEK 2,300. Down: trades per customer falling a further 10% in Q4; deposits below 8.5% of capital with no hike; a 2027 cost guide above 9% Swedish growth or a Danish budget rise on 22 January 2027; Q1 2027 brokerage yield below 10 basis points; income per customer below SEK 2,050. The dates are the monthly statistics (first week of each month), the preliminary FY2026 statement and 2027 guide (22 January 2027), and the Q1 2027 report (16 April 2027).

How this differs from the first set's closing note. The first set's base EPS was SEK 19.0, 20.3 and 22.0 to 22.5 for FY2026 to FY2028; mine is 19.1, 20.2 and 21.7. The difference in 2028 (minus 2% to 4%) comes from the lower revenue margin path and the heavier Danish phasing, both argued above; the direction and the shape are the same. I would also add a figure the first set did not give: the FY2030 EPS of about SEK 25.3, which at the stock's current 16.2x would be worth about SEK 410 four years out (plus about SEK 70 of cumulative dividends), and at the first set's fair range of 17x to 20x about SEK 430 to 505. The base case therefore supports a return in the low double digits a year from SEK 360 with no re-rating, which is the arithmetic the fourth set will test against guidance, consensus and narrative.

Change in the share count, other than through buybacks or new shares issued for cash (millions of shares) 0.6 0.6 0.6 0.6 0.6

Revenue growth is against the same period a year earlier. This company uses the operating profit form: a financial company (G. Financials; G.5. Digital investment and trading platforms). Operating profit is after depreciation and amortisation, except the amortisation of acquired intangibles, as adjusted earnings are. For banks and insurers, interest is part of the business, so operating profit is normally close to pre-tax profit.

Notes on Part 1. Revenue is the company's operating income (FY2025 base SEK 4,495m, the basis consensus uses), not FactSet's standardised revenue of SEK 5,737m, which includes gross interest and must not be used. Operating profit equals the company's operating profit and its reported pre-tax profit (no credit losses since 2011, no interest-bearing debt). "Net financial items" carries the coupon on SEK 1.3bn of perpetual Additional Tier 1 notes (STIBOR plus 3.25% on SEK 800m and plus 2.85% on SEK 500m), which under IFRS is charged to equity below pre-tax profit and reduces earnings attributable to ordinary shareholders; it is entered here so that the model's EPS is on the shareholder basis (about SEK 60m in 2026 at an average STIBOR near 1.9%, about SEK 70m a year from 2027 at STIBOR 2.25%; the coupon is not tax-deductible, so the model's tax on it overstates net income by about SEK 10m a year, which is immaterial). The tax rate is low because most of Avanza Pension's income bears yield tax charged to policyholders rather than corporate tax; 15% is the outer-year alternative if the bank's share of profit rises. The dividend is 75% of base-case EPS (policy at least 70%; 76% paid for 2025). The share-count change is warrant dilution of about 0.4% a year, capped at 1.6% across live programmes.

Acquisitions less disposals -6 0 0 0 0
Buybacks less new shares issued for cash 0 0 0 0 0

Capital spending, working capital and lease payments are left out of this form.

Notes on Part 2. The FY2026 acquisition figure is the final SEK 6.3m instalment for Sigmastocks paid in July 2026; Denmark is organic and no acquisition is in prospect. No buyback is in the base case: the 10% authorisation is renewed and unused, and management's stated tool for surplus capital is the payout ratio. Warrant exercises bring in small cash sums (SEK 16m to 27m a year in FactSet's history) that are left at zero here because they are below the model's resolution. For reference, capital expenditure runs at SEK 60m to 90m a year (SEK 74m in 2025) and development is expensed.

Notes on Part 3. The quarter to 31-Dec-26 assumes operating income of about SEK 1,290m (net interest income about SEK 480m with two months of a 25 basis point Riksbank hike from 4 November and lending growth; brokerage about SEK 310m; fund commissions about SEK 250m; currency about SEK 192m; other about SEK 58m) against costs of about SEK 430m (Q4 is the seasonal cost peak; SEK 402m in Q4 2025), giving operating profit of about SEK 860m. The 2027 quarters carry operating income of about SEK 1,390m, 1,395m, 1,390m and 1,425m against costs of about SEK 430m, 445m, 425m and 480m (the Danish launch and marketing fall in H2 2027); the low growth in the quarter to 30-Jun-27 reflects the Q2 2026 activity peak (operating income SEK 1,334m) as the comparison, and the low margin in the quarter to 31-Dec-27 reflects the Danish launch costs on top of the seasonal cost peak.

Part 4. The reasoning behind the inputs

Segments: revenue and margin by segment, where the company reports them

Avanza reports one segment, Savings and Investments, and does not report segment margins. The useful split is by revenue line, which the company discloses quarterly and which consensus forecasts. Base case (SEK m): net interest income 1,483 (FY2025), 1,850 (FY2026), 2,130 (FY2027), 2,280 (FY2028), 2,420 (FY2029), 2,560 (FY2030); net brokerage income 1,259, 1,325, 1,350, 1,430, 1,515, 1,605; fund commissions 764, 930, 1,060, 1,190, 1,330, 1,480; currency-related income 719, 815, 810, 850, 890, 935; other income 270, 245, 250, 275, 320, 380; total 4,495, 5,165, 5,600, 6,025, 6,475, 6,960. (The FY2025 split applies the company's stated 2025 mix of 33%, 28%, 17%, 16% and 6% to SEK 4,495m and is approximate to within about SEK 20m a line.) Costs: Sweden 1,413, 1,535, 1,650, 1,760, 1,870, 1,960; Denmark 0, 40, 130, 150, 175, 170; total 1,413, 1,575, 1,780, 1,910, 2,045, 2,130. Denmark is a cost line only through FY2030 (launch in H2 2027; management expects break-even about five years after launch); its first revenue, perhaps SEK 10m in 2028, SEK 40m in 2029 and SEK 50m in 2030, sits in "other income". If the model needs a two-segment view: Sweden operating profit 3,630 (FY2026), 3,950 (FY2027), 4,255 (FY2028), 4,565 (FY2029), 4,950 (FY2030) at margins of 70.3%, 70.5%, 70.7%, 70.9% and 71.6%; Denmark a loss of 40, 130, 140, 135 and 120.

The operating drivers behind the inputs

Savings capital (SEK bn, year-end): 1,079 (2025), about 1,270 (2026: SEK 1,277bn at 30 September, a flat risk-off fourth quarter assumed), 1,410 (2027), 1,560 (2028), 1,730 (2029), 1,910 (2030), from a 5% a year market return plus net inflows of about 6% of opening capital (SEK 78bn in 2026 after SEK 58.5bn in the first nine months, then 78, 88, 97 and 107); average capital 1,195, 1,340, 1,485, 1,645 and 1,820. Customers 2.44m (end-2026) growing 7.5% then 7%, 6.5% and 6%. The revenue margin on average savings capital falls from 44.2 basis points (FY2025) to 43.2 (2026), 41.8 (2027), 40.6 (2028), 39.4 (2029) and 38.2 (2030): about 0.8 basis points a year of price and mix erosion (brokerage yield from about 10.6 to 10.0 basis points of turnover as Private Banking and Pro customers take a rising share of trading; fund margin from 22.5 to 20 basis points as the index share of fund capital rises from 55.5%; the blended currency margin from 18 to 16 basis points as the 12.5 basis point manual currency account spreads among affluent customers) and about 0.4 basis points a year from market appreciation inflating the denominator. Trading velocity in 2027 at about 90% of the 2026 average (which included the H1 2026 peak), with the foreign share of turnover stable at 31% to 32%. Deposits about 8.5% to 9% of savings capital (SEK 113bn end-2026 rising to 146bn by 2030), interest-bearing share drifting up from 61%; one 25 basis point Riksbank hike to 2.0% on 4 November 2026 passed through to deposit rates at 60% to 70% with a one- to two-quarter lag (worth about SEK 160m of annual net interest income), three-month STIBOR 2.25% through 2027 to 2030, no second hike in the base; lending (SEK 38bn at September 2026) growing 12% to 15% a year within a loosening leverage ratio (4.8% in September 2026 against 3.5% required; about 5.2% to 5.5% by 2028). Costs: Swedish growth 9% in 2026 (guided), about 7.5% in 2027, 6.7%, 6.3% and 4.8% to 2030 (the framework is 8% average to 2030 and 5% in 2030), each year about 1% under the guide; Denmark SEK 120m to 150m pre-launch (20% capitalised), then running cost of SEK 80m and rising and marketing of up to SEK 60m a year for three years. Cost-to-average-savings capital falls from 13.9 to 11.7 basis points, which is why the operating margin holds at 68% to 69.5% while the revenue margin falls 6 basis points. Return on equity drifts from about 42% (2026) to 38% to 39% (2028 to 2030) at a 75% payout.

The company's guidance, and how your inputs compare with it

Avanza guides only on costs, capital and 2030 targets; there is no revenue or earnings guidance. Costs: Swedish cost growth of 9% in 2026 (reiterated for the fourth consecutive quarter on 16 October 2026) plus SEK 40m for Denmark (lowered from SEK 50m), so about SEK 1,580m; my FY2026 total of SEK 1,575m is 0.3% under, consistent with four years of guides met or beaten (2022 minus 2.7%, 2023 minus 0.7%, 2024 9.2% against 9.5%, 2025 10.4% against 11%). The 2030 framework (8% average annual Swedish cost growth 2025 to 2030, about 5% in 2030) is matched, with my path 1% under each year; Denmark follows the disclosed budget (SEK 120m to 150m pre-launch, SEK 80m running, up to SEK 60m marketing, five years to break-even) with phasing that puts about SEK 130m in 2027 and 150m to 175m a year in 2028 to 2030; the cloud budget (SEK 300m through 2030, expensed) is inside the Swedish line. The 2030 targets: savings capital of SEK 2,000bn (15% a year) is missed in my base (SEK 1,910bn, 10% to 11% a year), as it was in 2025 (13%); return on equity of at least 35% is met (38% to 42%); the 70% payout policy is exceeded (75%), as it was in 2025 (76%). The published rate sensitivity (SEK 420m of annual net interest income per percentage point of cuts; SEK 1.1bn per point of hikes, before pass-through) is used as stated. Consensus (FactSet median, 13 October 2026, before the Q3 print): revenue SEK 5,225m, 5,726m, 6,216m; EBIT SEK 3,629m, 3,978m, 4,274m (69.5%, 69.5%, 68.8%); EPS SEK 19.40, 20.87, 22.88; DPS SEK 14.52, 15.41, 16.93 for FY2026 to FY2028. My inputs are 1% to 3% below on revenue, 1% to 4% below on EBIT and 1.5% to 5% below on EPS, the difference coming from the revenue margin (consensus FY2027 revenue implies about 43 to 43.5 basis points against my 41.8) and from Danish cost phasing (consensus FY2027 costs of about SEK 1,748m implied against my SEK 1,780m). After the Q3 print DNB Carnegie cut to SEK 18.90, 19.29 and 20.79 (revenue SEK 5,174m and 5,440m for 2026 and 2027, net interest income SEK 1,835m and 2,042m, costs SEK 1,583m and 1,772m); my base sits between DNB Carnegie and the consensus on every line.

The bridge from the company's own reported figures to the basis consensus uses

Consensus revenue is the company's operating income (net interest income plus net commission income plus other income, after commission expenses), so the FY2025 base is SEK 4,495m, which is also FactSet's "brokers' basis" actual; FactSet's fundamentals revenue of SEK 5,737m includes gross interest income and does not reconcile (the financials block flags a 21.6% gap and says not to use it). Consensus EBIT is the company's operating profit (SEK 3,078m for FY2025), which equals reported pre-tax profit because there are no credit losses and no net interest expense of note; FactSet's history shows EBIT of SEK 3,084m and pre-tax of SEK 3,078m, a rounding difference. Consensus EPS is the brokers' adjusted figure, which differs from reported basic EPS by 0.2% (SEK 16.53 against 16.57 for FY2025) because the company has no adjusting items: development, cloud and Danish costs are expensed, the only acquisition (Sigmastocks, SEK 21m) is immaterial and its algorithm amortises at under SEK 2m a quarter. One broker difference to note: DNB Carnegie deducts the AT1 coupon from net income in its EPS (hence its lower figures), while FactSet does not state whether the consensus does; my cross-check deducts it. Consensus net income (SEK 3,091m for FY2026) equals consensus EPS times about 159m diluted shares. FactSet's "total debt" in consensus (SEK 118.8bn for FY2026) is customer deposits, not debt; FactSet's free cash flow (SEK minus 797m in 2025) is deposit and lending flow and is not a conversion measure, which is why the cross-check below is net income. Depreciation and amortisation (SEK 96m in 2025 on FactSet; about SEK 107m in 2027 on DNB Carnegie) is inside the company's operating costs and therefore inside the operating profit margin entered in Part 1.

Earnings per share 19.1 20.2 21.7 23.3 25.3
Net income 3,020 3,205 3,460 3,730 4,070

Derivation: revenue 5,165, 5,600, 6,025, 6,475, 6,960; costs 1,575, 1,780, 1,910, 2,045, 2,130; operating profit 3,590, 3,820, 4,115, 4,430, 4,830; less AT1 coupons 60, 70, 70, 70, 70; tax at 14.5%; net income to ordinary shareholders 3,020, 3,205, 3,460, 3,730, 4,070; weighted average shares 158.2m, 158.8m, 159.4m, 160.0m, 160.6m. EPS growth 15%, 6%, 7%, 8%, 9%; compound rate FY2025 to FY2030 about 8.8% a year. Scenarios beside the base for FY2027: an upside of about SEK 22.5 (H1 2026 trading velocity returning plus a second hike to 2.25%, probability about 25%) and a downside of about SEK 16.2 (a 2022-type activity year with costs still rising, probability about 25%).

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for Layer 3 and Layer 4

Research requests for the next set

Company: Avanza Bank Holding AB (AZA-SE), Sweden's largest digital savings and investment platform: 2.39 million retail, Private Banking and Pro customers and SEK 1,277bn of savings capital (30 September 2026) in one reported segment (Savings and Investments) across three regulated entities (Avanza Bank, Avanza Pension, Avanza Fonder); FY2025 operating income of SEK 4,495m split net interest income 33%, net brokerage 28%, fund commissions 17%, currency-related income 16%, other 6%; organic entry into Denmark planned for the second half of 2027.

Request: T-D1

Peers: Nordnet (SAVE-SE), the direct pan-Nordic peer on one platform across four countries, launching in Germany in November 2026 and the incumbent price-setter in Denmark; FinecoBank (FBK-IT), the precedent for a platform that decelerated cost growth over a multi-year plan while entering new markets and for net interest income stabilising after rate cuts; AJ Bell (AJB-GB), the precedent for sustaining double-digit asset growth through fee cuts and marketing against a fixed cost base; flatexDEGIRO (FTK-DE), the precedent for a transaction-heavy platform whose earnings normalised after a retail boom and that reset its personnel cost; Hargreaves Lansdown (private since 2025), the precedent for a market leader whose asset growth continued while revenue margin and earnings growth slowed; Swissquote (SQN-CH), a single-core platform that absorbed multi-market expansion costs; SAVR, Montrose and Lysa (private Swedish entrants), which price below the incumbents at the affluent and passive ends. Operating conditions: a Riksbank hiking cycle beginning from 1.75% (decision due 4 November 2026) after a cutting cycle from 4.0% in 2024 to 1.75% in 2025; a risk-off, low-volatility quarter following an activity peak (the third quarter of 2026 after the first half); risk-on periods in which customers move cash from deposits into funds and shares; a rising share of Private Banking and Pro customers in trading (30% in the third quarter of 2026) and of index funds in fund capital (55.5%); an expansion year in which a platform spends ahead of revenue on a new market (Denmark in 2027, as Nordnet in Germany); a strong krona; the EU Retail Investment Strategy's implementation to July 2029 with no inducement ban. Inputs: The expected change to test is a transition from mid-teens revenue and earnings growth (operating income +15% in 2024 and 2025, +15% in the first nine months of 2026; operating profit +17% in 2025) to a lower growth rate driven by savings-capital compounding while revenue per unit falls (brokerage yield 10.3 basis points of turnover in the third quarter of 2026 from 11.4 in the second quarter of 2025; fund margin 22.2 basis points from 23.3; income per customer SEK 2,170 annualised, down 6% quarter on quarter), with the cost line held to the company's framework (9% Swedish cost growth in 2026, 8% average to 2030, about 5% in 2030) through a Danish launch budgeted at SEK 120m to 150m pre-launch plus SEK 80m running and up to SEK 60m marketing a year. The record to test: cost guidance met or beaten in 2022, 2023, 2024 (9.2% against 9.5%) and 2025 (10.4% against 11%); the 15% savings-capital growth target missed in 2025 (13%); savings-capital market share rising from 7.8% (end-2024) to 8.7% (September 2026) with 11.9% of Swedish net inflows; quarterly results against the company-compiled consensus over the last three years, including the Q3 2026 operating profit 2% below consensus with every revenue line below and costs 3% better; the 2022 precedent (operating income down about 10%, costs up 19%, EPS down 19%). The strengths to test: cost discipline, customer retention (churn 0.7%), brand (16 consecutive Swedish Quality Index wins), low acquisition cost, order-flow scale (20% of Nasdaq Stockholm transactions), deposit pricing (75% to 80% pass-through of cuts in 2024 and 2025). The strengths it would need to acquire: yield management across fee classes and currency accounts; building and running a second technology platform in a second country; monetising a discretionary product (Avanza Sigma, launched 31 August 2026 at 0.55% to 0.85%, SEK 710m after one month); converting pension and Private Banking wallet share at a rate that lifts income per customer. Precedents sought: platforms that held operating margins near 70% through an expansion year; platforms whose revenue margin on assets fell while assets compounded (Hargreaves Lansdown after the UK Retail Distribution Review; Avanza itself 2021 to 2023); platforms that entered an incumbent's home market organically (Nordnet in Germany 2010 exit, Fineco in the UK to 2023, Nordnet in Denmark from 2001); platforms that sustained double-digit asset growth above 20% population penetration. Focus: Whether a platform with Avanza's demonstrated record (cost caps delivered, assets gathered, customers retained) has in any precedent converted asset compounding into sustained earnings growth while its revenue per unit was falling and it was funding a new-market launch, and at what rate, or whether the earnings growth in such periods has come from a rate or activity cycle rather than from the business's own levers.

Request: T-D2

Peers: Nordnet (SAVE-SE), the direct peer with published rate sensitivities, the same deposit and leverage-ratio dynamics and a Danish franchise earning a higher revenue margin; FinecoBank (FBK-IT), for the historical dispersion of a diversified platform's earnings around a rate cycle and for net interest income stabilising through volume; AJ Bell (AJB-GB), for a platform whose earnings depend on client cash interest and whose forecast dispersion is set by the base rate; flatexDEGIRO (FTK-DE), for the dispersion of a transaction-geared platform's earnings after an activity peak (2021 to 2023) and the accuracy of forecasts made at the peak; Hargreaves Lansdown (private), for the dispersion of outcomes when revenue margin, cash interest and inflows moved together; Integrafin (IHP-GB), for a platform with almost no rate or trading gearing as the low-dispersion comparator; Swissquote (SQN-CH), for a platform whose earnings swing with crypto and trading volumes. Operating conditions: a Riksbank hike from 1.75% (4 November 2026) with forward curves pricing further rises through 2027, against the cutting cycle of 2024 to 2025; quarters of falling retail trading velocity after a peak (Avanza's transactions down 8% quarter on quarter in the third quarter of 2026, Deutsche Bank's tracker showing trades down 10% year on year in September); risk-on quarters draining deposits (8.9% of savings capital in the third quarter of 2026 from 9.6%) and risk-off quarters rebuilding them; a Swedish equity market drawdown; a strong or weak krona against the dollar (foreign securities about 31% of brokerage-generating turnover, about 70% of it US); the Danish launch year (second half of 2027). Inputs: The drivers that separate the next 18 months to four years from the last four to six: (1) the revenue margin on savings capital by line (net interest income, brokerage, fund commissions, currency, other) and how far it moves per unit of change in the policy rate, trading velocity, the foreign share of turnover, the Private Banking and Pro share of trading and the index share of fund capital, with the company's own history by quarter since 2019; (2) the policy rate, with the company's published sensitivity of about SEK 420m of annual net interest income per percentage point of cuts and about SEK 1.1bn per point of hikes (third-quarter 2026 report) and brokers' pass-through assumptions; (3) trading velocity and its relationship to realised volatility, with the 2021 to 2023 normalisation as the precedent (EPS SEK 13.19 in 2021, SEK 10.69 in 2022, SEK 12.64 in 2023); (4) the deposit base's response to risk appetite; (5) the cost line, guided and delivered, plus the Danish budget by year; (6) savings capital from market return and net inflow (net inflows SEK 90bn in 2021, 24bn in 2022, 72bn in 2023, 86bn in 2024, 54bn in 2025, SEK 58.5bn in the first nine months of 2026). How concentrated are these: what share of the variance in Avanza's quarterly operating profit since 2019 is explained by the policy rate and by turnover per customer alone? The forecast differences to test: the FactSet consensus of 13 October 2026 (revenue SEK 5,225m, 5,726m and 6,216m; EPS SEK 19.40, 20.87 and 22.88 for 2026 to 2028) against the dispersion of individual brokers (FY2027 EPS from SEK 19.34 at DNB Carnegie to SEK 22.85 at Deutsche Bank; revenue SEK 5,440m to 6,054m), and which line explains the spread. Historical dispersion: how wide was the range of Avanza's realised annual EPS against the consensus made twelve months earlier in each year since 2019, and the same for Nordnet, flatexDEGIRO, AJ Bell and FinecoBank; which events widened it (2020 to 2021 boom, 2022 normalisation, 2023 rate peak, 2024 to 2025 cuts) and which narrowed it; how often forecasts that rested mainly on one driver (a rate path or an activity level) were met. Focus: How much of the range of Avanza's plausible earnings outcomes over the next 18 months is explained by two inputs alone (the Riksbank path and turnover per customer), how wide that range has been historically in quarters that followed an activity peak and preceded a rate turn, and whether the distribution around consensus in such periods has been symmetric or skewed to misses.

Request: T-I1

Peers: Nordnet (SAVE-SE), which reported its third quarter on 22 October 2026 and guides on cost growth and a leverage-ratio target of 4.0% to 4.5%, and whose German launch in November 2026 is the sector's other expansion story; FinecoBank (FBK-IT), which has a record of pulling targets forward and guiding costs down; AJ Bell (AJB-GB), whose full-year results on 3 December 2026 and base-rate guidance set the read-across for cash-interest-dependent platforms; flatexDEGIRO (FTK-DE), downgraded by Deutsche Bank in October 2026 after a top-line miss with full-year guidance unchanged, and due to set targets in February 2027; Integrafin (IHP-GB), with costs capped at 3% a year for 2026 and 2027, as the comparator for how the market rewards a cost cap; Swissquote (SQN-CH) and IG Group (IGG-GB), for how trading-geared platforms' guidance has moved in the risk-off quarter. Operating conditions: the Riksbank decision of 4 November 2026 and its path through 2027; a risk-off regime with falling retail trading velocity following the first-half 2026 peak; the 2027 cost guides that the sector issues in January and February 2027 (Avanza's preliminary full-year statement and dividend proposal on 22 January 2027); the Danish pre-launch disclosures in the first half of 2027 and the launch in the second half; the EU Retail Investment Strategy's final text (expected January 2027); Sweden's post-election debate on a bank tax, ISK taxation and endowment-insurance interest deductibility. Inputs: Avanza's guidance consists only of a Swedish cost-growth figure (9% for 2026, reiterated on 16 October 2026 for the fourth consecutive quarter), a Danish cost figure (SEK 40m for 2026, lowered from SEK 50m in October 2026, with the total pre-launch budget of SEK 120m to 150m unchanged), a cloud budget (SEK 300m through 2030), 2030 targets (SEK 2,000bn of savings capital, 8% average cost growth, at least 35% return on equity, 70% payout) and a published rate sensitivity; it gives no revenue or earnings guidance and compiles its own consensus before each quarter (18 contributors for the third quarter of 2026). The dynamics to test: (1) the record of the cost guide (beaten in 2022, 2023, 2024 and 2025) and what the 2027 Swedish cost guide of 22 January 2027 is likely to be, given management's statements that headcount growth will slow in 2027 and that 2025 and 2026 are investment years; (2) whether the Danish budget is likely to be raised, held or cut at the January 2027 and April 2027 reports, and what Nordnet's German launch costs (guided at SEK 200m to 230m over three years per Jefferies, 8 July 2026) imply; (3) where consensus revenue sits against the company's run rate after the third quarter (operating income SEK 1,283m, 2% below the company-compiled consensus of SEK 1,310m, with brokerage 5%, currency 8%, fund commissions 3% and net interest income 2% below) and what the fourth-quarter monthly statistics (published in the first week of each month) have shown since; (4) the sell-side's revisions since 16 October 2026 (DNB Carnegie to FY2026 EPS SEK 18.90 from 19.04 and target SEK 425 from 430; Deutsche Bank holding SEK 440; Jefferies holding SEK 335 Underperform; the FactSet FY2027 EPS consensus up 1% over one month and 13% over twelve as of 22 October 2026; buy share 50%) and which brokers have yet to update; (5) the direction of consensus net interest income (FactSet SEK 1,877m, 2,197m, 2,417m for 2026 to 2028) if the Riksbank hikes on 4 November and if it does not; (6) the dividend proposal for 2025 earnings (consensus DPS about SEK 14.5 against a 70% policy and 76% paid for 2025) and whether the 10% buyback authorisation is expected to be used; (7) peer read-across from Nordnet's third quarter (operating profit +24% year on year, a slight miss on margins per DNB Carnegie, 23 October 2026), AJ Bell's 3 December results and flatexDEGIRO's February 2027 targets; (8) the 2030 savings-capital target of SEK 2,000bn (15% a year; 13% delivered in 2025; 21% year on year at September 2026 with market help) and whether brokers expect it to be reaffirmed, reframed or dropped at the next capital markets day. Focus: Whether the sell-side's FY2027 revenue and earnings estimates, which rose through 2026 and had not been cut after the third-quarter miss, are more likely to be cut or held over the fourth-quarter report (22 January 2027) and the first-quarter report (16 April 2027), given the monthly trading and deposit statistics and the Riksbank's November decision, and whether the 2027 cost guide can absorb the Danish launch inside the 8% framework.

Request: T-I2

Peers: Nordnet (SAVE-SE), whose growth, buybacks, Shareville engagement and German launch set the comparison in Nordic investor commentary; FinecoBank (FBK-IT), AJ Bell (AJB-GB) and Integrafin (IHP-GB), the platforms whose commentary centres on the shift from cash interest to asset-scaled fees; flatexDEGIRO (FTK-DE), whose derating in 2026 shows how the market treats a platform read as ex-growth; Hargreaves Lansdown (private), the reference case in UK commentary for yield compression at a market leader; SAVR, Montrose and Lysa (private), which feature in Swedish retail-investor discussion of fees. Operating conditions: the quarter after an activity peak in a risk-off regime; a Riksbank turn from cuts to hikes; a sector trading near the bottom of its ten-year multiple range (online brokers and platforms at the 20th percentile per FactSet, 12 October 2026; Avanza at about the 12th to 17th percentile) after a run of first-half beats that did not re-rate; Nordnet's German launch (November 2026) and Avanza's Danish launch (second half of 2027) as the two expansion stories; the EU Retail Investment Strategy's final vote and Sweden's bank-tax debate. Inputs: The dynamics whose articulation to test: (1) the fall in revenue per unit in 2026 (brokerage yield 10.3 basis points, fund margin 22.2 basis points, currency margin 18 basis points on foreign turnover, income per customer SEK 2,170 annualised) and the debate over whether it is cyclical (management: "temporary market sentiment"; DNB Carnegie: seasonal but with Private Banking competition a concern) or structural (Jefferies: structurally lower income per customer; Morgan Stanley in 2023: a 45 basis point platform yield unsustainable); (2) the fixed-cost structure (personnel about 60% of costs, headcount up 15% to 812) and the 2027 Danish cost phasing, and how commentators treat the 2027 cost and margin outlook; (3) the asymmetric rate sensitivity (SEK 420m per point down, SEK 1.1bn per point up) and the November 2026 hike as an earnings tailwind; (4) the deposit and risk-appetite loop (deposits 8.9% of savings capital, down from 9.6%, as customers bought funds) and the leverage ratio (4.8% against 3.5%) as the constraint on lending, payout and buybacks, including the third-quarter mortgage application pause for customers with SEK 3m to 10m; (5) the passive-mix shift (index funds 55.5% of fund capital; own funds 41%) and the Retail Investment Strategy's value-for-money tests; (6) the Danish entry (budget, separate AI-built platform, Nordnet as price-setter with a 49 to 50 basis point Danish revenue margin, break-even in about five years) and whether it is discussed as an option, a cost or a distraction; (7) the relative growth gap to Nordnet (customers +8% against +12%; savings capital +21% against +28%; Nordnet's buybacks against Avanza's unused authorisation); (8) Avanza Sigma (SEK 710m after one month at half the incumbent price) and occupational pensions (11.1% unit-linked premium share, the transfer-signature petition) as wallet-share levers; (9) the possibility that Avanza Bank's liabilities (customer deposits SEK 111.9bn at September 2026) approach the threshold for Sweden's risk tax on credit institutions (SEK 150bn when introduced in 2022, indexed since) within a few years; (10) platform reliability and outages under load, and the Google Cloud migration. For each: which analysts (DNB Carnegie, Deutsche Bank, Jefferies, Morgan Stanley, Barclays, Nordea, Pareto, UBS, Goldman Sachs, Keefe Bruyette & Woods, SEB, Handelsbanken, ABG Sundal Collier, Carnegie) and which investor commentators (on X/Twitter, Placera and Avanza's own forum, Redeye, Börsvärlden, Swedish financial press such as Dagens Industri and Affärsvärlden, and international platform specialists) discuss it, how prominently, since when and with what view. Focus: Whether the market narrative on Avanza after the third quarter of 2026 has absorbed the idea that savings-capital growth and earnings growth have decoupled (strong flows, weaker monetisation, a fixed cost line with a Danish bill), or whether commentary still frames the stock as a rate and trading-volume play whose next move depends on the Riksbank and on volatility; and which of the ten dynamics above is discussed by no one.
